## G10C01 — Perimeter

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C01", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MEN-ARC", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S1", "MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S2", "MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S3", "MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Perimeter · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-ARC`

**Question:** A sector has radius 7 cm and centre angle 90°. Find its perimeter. (Take π = 22/7.)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Perimeter of a sector includes the two straight radii.
- **Hint 2:** First find the arc: 2πr × 90/360.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the arc length formula. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S1 -->

$$l = 2\pi r \times \frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute r and θ. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S2 -->

$$l = 2\times\frac{22}{7}\times 7\times\frac{90}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify the arc length. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S3 -->

$$l = 11\ \text{cm}$$

Step 4 — Perimeter = arc + two radii. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-01-S4 -->

$$P = 11 + 7 + 7 = 25\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $25$ cm
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $25$ · **Unit:** cm

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Forgetting to add the two radii.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Topic:** Perimeter · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-ARC`

**Question:** A sector has radius 14 cm and centre angle 180°. Find its perimeter. (Take π = 22/7.)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Perimeter of a sector includes the two straight radii.
- **Hint 2:** First find the arc: 2πr × 180/360.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the arc length formula. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-02-S1 -->

$$l = 2\pi r \times \frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute r and θ. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-02-S2 -->

$$l = 2\times\frac{22}{7}\times 14\times\frac{180}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify the arc length. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-02-S3 -->

$$l = 44\ \text{cm}$$

Step 4 — Perimeter = arc + two radii. <!-- MQ-G10C01-F-SECTOR_PER-02-S4 -->

$$P = 44 + 14 + 14 = 72\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $72$ cm
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $72$ · **Unit:** cm

**Step-by-step solution**

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C01", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MEN-CIRC", "diagram": "GEO-COMPOSITE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S1", "MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S2", "MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S3", "MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Perimeter · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-MEN-CIRC`

**Question:** A shape is a rectangle 20 cm long and 14 cm wide, with a semicircle drawn outward on one 14 cm side (that side is removed). Find the perimeter of the shape. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-COMPOSITE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-COMPOSITE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Trace the outline: which edges are on the outside?
- **Hint 2:** The side under the semicircle is NOT part of the perimeter.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Radius of the semicircle = half of the width. <!-- MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S1 -->

$$r = 14\div 2 = 7\ \text{cm}$$

Step 2 — Curved edge = half the circumference. <!-- MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S2 -->

$$\pi r = \frac{22}{7}\times 7 = 22\ \text{cm}$$

Step 3 — Add the straight edges: two lengths and one width. <!-- MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S3 -->

$$20+20+14 = 54\ \text{cm}$$

Step 4 — Total perimeter. <!-- MQ-G10C01-G-RECT_SEMIC-01-S4 -->

$$P = 54 + 22 = 76\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $76$ cm
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $76$ · **Unit:** cm

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Including the hidden side under the semicircle.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C01", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-ARC", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S1", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S2", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S3", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Perimeter · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-ARC`

**Question:** The perimeter of a sector of radius 7 cm is 36 cm. Find the angle at the centre. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Arc = perimeter − 2r.
- **Hint 2:** θ/360 = arc ÷ full circumference.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Subtract the two radii to get the arc. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S1 -->

$$l = 36 - 2\times 7 = 22$$

Step 2 — Use the arc formula. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S2 -->

$$22 = 2\times\frac{22}{7}\times 7\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 3 — Find the full circumference. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S3 -->

$$2\pi r = 44$$

Step 4 — Solve for θ. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-01-S4 -->

$$\theta = \frac{22}{44}\times 360 = 180^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $180^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $180^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Dividing the whole perimeter by the circumference without removing 2r.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C01", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-ARC", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S1", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S2", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S3", "MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S4"]} -->

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Arc = perimeter − 2r.
- **Hint 2:** θ/360 = arc ÷ full circumference.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Subtract the two radii to get the arc. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S1 -->

$$l = 25 - 2\times 7 = 11$$

Step 2 — Use the arc formula. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S2 -->

$$11 = 2\times\frac{22}{7}\times 7\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 3 — Find the full circumference. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S3 -->

$$2\pi r = 44$$

Step 4 — Solve for θ. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-02-S4 -->

$$\theta = \frac{11}{44}\times 360 = 90^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $90^\circ$

**Question:** The perimeter of a sector of radius 21 cm is 64 cm. Find the angle at the centre. (π = 22/7)

- **Hint 1:** Arc = perimeter − 2r.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 2 — Use the arc formula. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-03-S2 -->

$$22 = 2\times\frac{22}{7}\times 21\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 3 — Find the full circumference. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-03-S3 -->

$$2\pi r = 132$$

Step 4 — Solve for θ. <!-- MQ-G10C01-A-SECTOR_ANG-03-S4 -->

$$\theta = \frac{22}{132}\times 360 = 60^\circ$$

## G10C02 — Square Root

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C02", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-SQRT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S1", "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S2", "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Square Root · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-SQRT`

**Question:** Find √1296 using prime factors.

- **Hint 1:** Divide by 2, 3, 5, 7 … repeatedly.
- **Hint 2:** Each prime appears an even number of times in a perfect square.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the number as a product of prime factors, in pairs. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S1 -->

$$1296 = (2\times 2)\times(2\times 2)\times(3\times 3)\times(3\times 3)$$

Step 2 — Take one factor from each pair. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S2 -->

$$\sqrt{1296} = 2\times2\times3\times3$$

Step 3 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-01-S3 -->

$$\sqrt{1296} = 36$$

**Final answer:** $36$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $36$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C02", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-SQRT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S1", "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S2", "MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Square Root · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-SQRT`

**Question:** Find √1764 using prime factors.

- **Hint 1:** Divide by 2, 3, 5, 7 … repeatedly.
- **Hint 2:** Each prime appears an even number of times in a perfect square.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the number as a product of prime factors, in pairs. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S1 -->

$$1764 = (2\times 2)\times(3\times 3)\times(7\times 7)$$

Step 2 — Take one factor from each pair. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S2 -->

$$\sqrt{1764} = 2\times3\times7$$

Step 3 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C02-F-SQRT_PRIME-02-S3 -->

$$\sqrt{1764} = 42$$

**Final answer:** $42$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $42$

#### MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C02", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-NUM-SQRT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S1", "MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S2", "MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S3", "MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Square Root · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-NUM-SQRT`

**Question:** Find the first approximation (correct to the first decimal place) of √85.

- **Hint 1:** Between which two whole numbers is it?
- **Hint 2:** Square numbers like a.1, a.2, … and see which is closest.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find the perfect squares on either side. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S1 -->

$$9^2=81 < 85 < 100=10^2 \Rightarrow 9 < \sqrt{85} < 10$$

Step 2 — Try the nearest one-decimal value by squaring. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S2 -->

$$9.2^2 = 84.64$$

Step 3 — Check the half-way values. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S3 -->

$$9.15^2 = 83.7225 < 85 < 85.5625 = 9.25^2$$

Step 4 — So the square root rounds to: <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-01-S4 -->

$$\sqrt{85} \approx 9.2$$

**Final answer:** $9.2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $9.2$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Choosing the decimal whose square is just below N without checking which is closer.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

$$3^2=9 < 10 < 16=4^2 \Rightarrow 3 < \sqrt{10} < 4$$

Step 2 — Try the nearest one-decimal value by squaring. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-02-S2 -->

$$3.2^2 = 10.24$$

Step 3 — Check the half-way values. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-02-S3 -->

$$3.15^2 = 9.9225 < 10 < 10.5625 = 3.25^2$$

Step 4 — So the square root rounds to: <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-02-S4 -->

**Question:** Find the first approximation (correct to the first decimal place) of √5.

- **Hint 1:** Between which two whole numbers is it?
- **Hint 2:** Square numbers like a.1, a.2, … and see which is closest.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find the perfect squares on either side. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-04-S1 -->

$$2^2=4 < 5 < 9=3^2 \Rightarrow 2 < \sqrt{5} < 3$$

Step 2 — Try the nearest one-decimal value by squaring. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-04-S2 -->

$$2.2^2 = 4.84$$

Step 3 — Check the half-way values. <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-04-S3 -->

$$2.15^2 = 4.6225 < 5 < 5.0625 = 2.25^2$$

Step 4 — So the square root rounds to: <!-- MQ-G10C02-G-SQRT_FIRST-04-S4 -->

$$\sqrt{5} \approx 2.2$$

**Final answer:** $2.2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $2.2$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C02", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-SQRT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S1", "MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S2", "MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Square Root · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-SQRT`

**Question:** Find √0.0144 without a calculator.

- **Hint 1:** Count decimal places: the root has half as many.
- **Hint 2:** What is √144?

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the decimal as a fraction with a square denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S1 -->

$$0.0144 = \frac{144}{10000}$$

Step 2 — Take the square root of top and bottom separately. <!-- MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S2 -->

$$\sqrt{\frac{144}{10000}} = \frac{12}{100}$$

Step 3 — Write as a decimal. <!-- MQ-G10C02-A-SQRT_DECIM-01-S3 -->

$$= 0.12$$

**Final answer:** $0.12$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $0.12$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing too many decimal places, e.g. √0.09 = 0.03 (wrong; it is 0.3).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C03 — Fractions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S1", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S2", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S3", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $2\tfrac{1}{3} + 1\tfrac{1}{6}$

- **Hint 1:** Make the denominators equal first.
- **Hint 2:** LCM of 3 and 6 is 6.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Change mixed numbers to improper fractions. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S1 -->

$$2\tfrac{1}{3} = \frac{7}{3},\quad 1\tfrac{1}{6} = \frac{7}{6}$$

Step 2 — Use the common denominator 6. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{7}{3} + \frac{7}{6} = \frac{14}{6} + \frac{7}{6}$$

Step 3 — Add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S3 -->

$$= \frac{21}{6} = \frac{7}{2}$$

Step 4 — Write as a mixed number. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-01-S4 -->

$$= 3\tfrac{1}{2}$$

**Final answer:** $3\tfrac{1}{2}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3\tfrac{1}{2}$, $\frac{7}{2}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Adding denominators together (e.g. 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5 is wrong).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S1", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S2", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S3", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $3\tfrac{1}{2} + 1\tfrac{1}{5}$

- **Hint 1:** Make the denominators equal first.
- **Hint 2:** LCM of 2 and 5 is 10.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Change mixed numbers to improper fractions. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S1 -->

$$3\tfrac{1}{2} = \frac{7}{2},\quad 1\tfrac{1}{5} = \frac{6}{5}$$

Step 2 — Use the common denominator 10. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{7}{2} + \frac{6}{5} = \frac{35}{10} + \frac{12}{10}$$

Step 3 — Add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S3 -->

$$= \frac{47}{10} = \frac{47}{10}$$

Step 4 — Write as a mixed number. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-02-S4 -->

$$= 4\tfrac{7}{10}$$

**Final answer:** $4\tfrac{7}{10}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $4\tfrac{7}{10}$, $\frac{47}{10}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Adding denominators together (e.g. 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5 is wrong).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S1", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S2", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S3", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S4"]} -->

**Question:** Simplify: $2\tfrac{1}{3} + 2\tfrac{2}{5}$

- **Hint 1:** Make the denominators equal first.
- **Hint 2:** LCM of 3 and 5 is 15.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Change mixed numbers to improper fractions. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S1 -->

$$2\tfrac{1}{3} = \frac{7}{3},\quad 2\tfrac{2}{5} = \frac{12}{5}$$

Step 2 — Use the common denominator 15. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S2 -->

$$\frac{7}{3} + \frac{12}{5} = \frac{35}{15} + \frac{36}{15}$$

Step 3 — Add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S3 -->

$$= \frac{71}{15} = \frac{71}{15}$$

Step 4 — Write as a mixed number. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-03-S4 -->

$$= 4\tfrac{11}{15}$$

**Final answer:** $4\tfrac{11}{15}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $4\tfrac{11}{15}$, $\frac{71}{15}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Adding denominators together (e.g. 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5 is wrong).

#### MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S1", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S2", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S3", "MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S4"]} -->

**Question:** Simplify: $2\tfrac{1}{2} + 1\tfrac{1}{6}$

- **Hint 1:** Make the denominators equal first.
- **Hint 2:** LCM of 2 and 6 is 6.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Change mixed numbers to improper fractions. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S1 -->

$$2\tfrac{1}{2} = \frac{5}{2},\quad 1\tfrac{1}{6} = \frac{7}{6}$$

Step 2 — Use the common denominator 6. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S2 -->

$$\frac{5}{2} + \frac{7}{6} = \frac{15}{6} + \frac{7}{6}$$

Step 3 — Add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S3 -->

$$= \frac{22}{6} = \frac{11}{3}$$

Step 4 — Write as a mixed number. <!-- MQ-G10C03-F-ADD_MIXED-04-S4 -->

$$= 3\tfrac{2}{3}$$

**Final answer:** $3\tfrac{2}{3}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3\tfrac{2}{3}$, $\frac{11}{3}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Adding denominators together (e.g. 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5 is wrong).

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S1", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S2", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S3", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** A man spent \frac{1}{3} of his salary of Rs. 120 000 on food and \frac{1}{5} of the REMAINDER on education. How much money is left?

- **Hint 1:** 'Of the remainder' means use the money left after food.
- **Hint 2:** Work in stages: food → remainder → education → left.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Money for food. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S1 -->

$$120\,000\times \frac{1}{3} = 40\,000$$

Step 2 — Remainder after food. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S2 -->

$$120\,000 - 40\,000 = 80\,000$$

Step 3 — Education is a fraction of the remainder. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S3 -->

$$80\,000\times \frac{1}{5} = 16\,000$$

Step 4 — Money left. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-01-S4 -->

$$80\,000 - 16\,000 = 64\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }64\,000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }64\,000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Taking the second fraction of the whole salary instead of the remainder.

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S1", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S2", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S3", "MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** A man spent \frac{1}{3} of his salary of Rs. 60 000 on food and \frac{2}{5} of the REMAINDER on education. How much money is left?

- **Hint 1:** 'Of the remainder' means use the money left after food.
- **Hint 2:** Work in stages: food → remainder → education → left.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Money for food. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S1 -->

$$60\,000\times \frac{1}{3} = 20\,000$$

Step 2 — Remainder after food. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S2 -->

$$60\,000 - 20\,000 = 40\,000$$

Step 3 — Education is a fraction of the remainder. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S3 -->

$$40\,000\times \frac{2}{5} = 16\,000$$

Step 4 — Money left. <!-- MQ-G10C03-G-FRACTION_R-02-S4 -->

$$40\,000 - 16\,000 = 24\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }24\,000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }24\,000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Taking the second fraction of the whole salary instead of the remainder.

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S1", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S2", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S3", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** From a plot of land, \frac{1}{5} was used for a house and \frac{1}{3} of the remaining part for vegetables. The rest, 480 m², was grassland. (i) What fraction of the whole land is grassland? (ii) Find the total area of the land.

- **Hint 1:** Convert every part into a fraction of the WHOLE land.
- **Hint 2:** If (fraction) × whole = area, divide the area by the fraction.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Fraction remaining after the house. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S1 -->

$$1 - \frac{1}{5} = \frac{4}{5}$$

Step 2 — Vegetable fraction of the whole land. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{4}{5}\times \frac{1}{3} = \frac{4}{15}$$

Step 3 — Grassland fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{4}{5} - \frac{4}{15} = \frac{8}{15}$$

Step 4 — That fraction equals the given area, so find the whole. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{8}{15}\ \text{of land} = 480 \Rightarrow \text{land} = 480\div \frac{8}{15} = 900\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ \frac{8}{15}\quad (ii)\ 900\ \text{m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ \frac{8}{15}\quad (ii)\ 900\ \text{m}^2$ · **Unit:** m²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying the area by the fraction instead of dividing.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S1", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S2", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S3", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** From a plot of land, \frac{1}{4} was used for a house and \frac{2}{3} of the remaining part for vegetables. The rest, 150 m², was grassland. (i) What fraction of the whole land is grassland? (ii) Find the total area of the land.

- **Hint 1:** Convert every part into a fraction of the WHOLE land.
- **Hint 2:** If (fraction) × whole = area, divide the area by the fraction.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Fraction remaining after the house. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S1 -->

$$1 - \frac{1}{4} = \frac{3}{4}$$

Step 2 — Vegetable fraction of the whole land. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{3}{4}\times \frac{2}{3} = \frac{1}{2}$$

Step 3 — Grassland fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S3 -->

$$\frac{3}{4} - \frac{1}{2} = \frac{1}{4}$$

Step 4 — That fraction equals the given area, so find the whole. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-02-S4 -->

$$\frac{1}{4}\ \text{of land} = 150 \Rightarrow \text{land} = 150\div \frac{1}{4} = 600\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ \frac{1}{4}\quad (ii)\ 600\ \text{m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ \frac{1}{4}\quad (ii)\ 600\ \text{m}^2$ · **Unit:** m²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying the area by the fraction instead of dividing.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C03", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-FRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S1", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S2", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S3", "MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-FRAC`

**Question:** From a plot of land, \frac{1}{5} was used for a house and \frac{2}{3} of the remaining part for vegetables. The rest, 320 m², was grassland. (i) What fraction of the whole land is grassland? (ii) Find the total area of the land.

- **Hint 1:** Convert every part into a fraction of the WHOLE land.
- **Hint 2:** If (fraction) × whole = area, divide the area by the fraction.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Fraction remaining after the house. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S1 -->

$$1 - \frac{1}{5} = \frac{4}{5}$$

Step 2 — Vegetable fraction of the whole land. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S2 -->

$$\frac{4}{5}\times \frac{2}{3} = \frac{8}{15}$$

Step 3 — Grassland fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S3 -->

$$\frac{4}{5} - \frac{8}{15} = \frac{4}{15}$$

Step 4 — That fraction equals the given area, so find the whole. <!-- MQ-G10C03-A-FRACTION_F-03-S4 -->

$$\frac{4}{15}\ \text{of land} = 320 \Rightarrow \text{land} = 320\div \frac{4}{15} = 1200\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ \frac{4}{15}\quad (ii)\ 1200\ \text{m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ \frac{4}{15}\quad (ii)\ 1200\ \text{m}^2$ · **Unit:** m²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying the area by the fraction instead of dividing.

## G10C04 — Binomial Expressions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01-S1", "MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand: $(x + 3)^2$

- **Hint 1:** Square of a binomial has THREE terms.
- **Hint 2:** Middle term = 2 × first × second.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Use (a ± b)² = a² ± 2ab + b². <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01-S1 -->

$$(x + 3)^2 = x^2 + 2\times x\times 3 + 3^2$$

Step 2 — Simplify each term. <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-01-S2 -->

$$= x^{2} + 6x + 9$$

**Final answer:** $x^{2} + 6x + 9$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing (x + a)² = x² + a² (missing the middle term).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Use (a ± b)² = a² ± 2ab + b². <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-02-S1 -->

$$(x + 6)^2 = x^2 + 2\times x\times 6 + 6^2$$

Step 2 — Simplify each term. <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-02-S2 -->

$$= x^{2} + 12x + 36$$

**Final answer:** $x^{2} + 12x + 36$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing (x + a)² = x² + a² (missing the middle term).

#### MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03-S1", "MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand: $(x - 4)^2$

- **Hint 1:** Square of a binomial has THREE terms.
- **Hint 2:** Middle term = 2 × first × second.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Use (a ± b)² = a² ± 2ab + b². <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03-S1 -->

$$(x - 4)^2 = x^2 - 2\times x\times 4 + 4^2$$

Step 2 — Simplify each term. <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-03-S2 -->

$$= x^{2} - 8x + 16$$

**Final answer:** $x^{2} - 8x + 16$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing (x + a)² = x² + a² (missing the middle term).

Step 2 — Simplify each term. <!-- MQ-G10C04-F-SQUARE_BIN-04-S2 -->

#### MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S1", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S2", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand and simplify: $(3x - 1)(2x + 5)$

- **Hint 1:** Four products: first×first, first×last, last×first, last×last.
- **Hint 2:** Watch the signs of negative numbers.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply each term of the first bracket by the second bracket. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S1 -->

$$3x(2x + 5)  - 1(2x + 5)$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S2 -->

$$= 6x^2  + 15x  - 2x  - 5$$

Step 3 — Collect like terms. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-01-S3 -->

$$= 6x^{2} + 13x - 5$$

**Final answer:** $6x^{2} + 13x - 5$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Sign errors when multiplying two negatives.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S1", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S2", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand and simplify: $(2x - 1)(2x + 5)$

- **Hint 1:** Four products: first×first, first×last, last×first, last×last.
- **Hint 2:** Watch the signs of negative numbers.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply each term of the first bracket by the second bracket. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S1 -->

$$2x(2x + 5)  - 1(2x + 5)$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S2 -->

$$= 4x^2  + 10x  - 2x  - 5$$

Step 3 — Collect like terms. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-02-S3 -->

$$= 4x^{2} + 8x - 5$$

**Final answer:** $4x^{2} + 8x - 5$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Sign errors when multiplying two negatives.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S1", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S2", "MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand and simplify: $(2x - 1)(x - 3)$

- **Hint 1:** Four products: first×first, first×last, last×first, last×last.
- **Hint 2:** Watch the signs of negative numbers.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply each term of the first bracket by the second bracket. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S1 -->

$$2x(x - 3)  - 1(x - 3)$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S2 -->

$$= 2x^2  - 6x  - 1x  + 3$$

Step 3 — Collect like terms. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-03-S3 -->

$$= 2x^{2} - 7x + 3$$

**Final answer:** $2x^{2} - 7x + 3$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Sign errors when multiplying two negatives.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-04

**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Expand and simplify: $(2x - 5)(x - 2)$

- **Hint 1:** Four products: first×first, first×last, last×first, last×last.
- **Hint 2:** Watch the signs of negative numbers.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply each term of the first bracket by the second bracket. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-04-S1 -->

$$2x(x - 2)  - 5(x - 2)$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-04-S2 -->

$$= 2x^2  - 4x  - 5x  + 10$$

Step 3 — Collect like terms. <!-- MQ-G10C04-G-PRODUCT_BI-04-S3 -->

$$= 2x^{2} - 9x + 10$$

**Final answer:** $2x^{2} - 9x + 10$

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C04", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-SQ", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S1", "MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S2", "MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S3", "MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Binomial Expressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SQ`

**Question:** Using a binomial expansion, find the value of $49^2$ without long multiplication.

- **Hint 1:** 49 is close to 50.
- **Hint 2:** (a ± b)² = a² ± 2ab + b²

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write 49 as 50 - 1. <!-- MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S1 -->

$$49^2 = (50 - 1)^2$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S2 -->

$$= 50^2 - 2\times50\times1 + 1^2$$

Step 3 — Calculate each term. <!-- MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S3 -->

$$= 2500 - 100 + 1$$

Step 4 — Add. <!-- MQ-G10C04-A-BINOMIAL_M-01-S4 -->

$$= 2401$$

**Final answer:** $2401$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $2401$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Forgetting the b² term at the end.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C05 — Congruence of Triangles

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C05", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-CONG", "diagram": null, "kind": "concept", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01-S1", "MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Congruence of Triangles · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-CONG`

**Question:** In triangles ABC and PQR: AB = PQ, ∠B = ∠Q, BC = QR. Which condition shows ΔABC ≡ ΔPQR?

- **Hint 1:** S = side, A = angle. Is the angle BETWEEN the two sides?
- **Hint 2:** A right angle with hypotenuse → RHS.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — List the equal parts in order around the triangle. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01-S1 -->

$$\text{AB = PQ, ∠B = ∠Q, BC = QR}$$

Step 2 — Match the pattern to a condition. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-01-S2 -->

$$\Rightarrow \text{SAS}$$

**Final answer:** $SAS (ப.கோ.ப)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using AAA — equal angles alone do not prove congruence.

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-02

**Topic:** Congruence of Triangles · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-CONG`

**Question:** In triangles ABC and PQR: ∠A = ∠P, AB = PQ, ∠B = ∠Q. Which condition shows ΔABC ≡ ΔPQR?

- **Hint 1:** S = side, A = angle. Is the angle BETWEEN the two sides?
- **Hint 2:** A right angle with hypotenuse → RHS.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — List the equal parts in order around the triangle. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-02-S1 -->

$$\text{∠A = ∠P, AB = PQ, ∠B = ∠Q}$$

Step 2 — Match the pattern to a condition. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-02-S2 -->

$$\Rightarrow \text{ASA}$$

**Final answer:** $ASA (கோ.ப.கோ)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using AAA — equal angles alone do not prove congruence.

**Question:** In triangles ABC and PQR: ∠B = ∠Q = 90°, AC = PR (hypotenuse), AB = PQ. Which condition shows ΔABC ≡ ΔPQR?

- **Hint 1:** S = side, A = angle. Is the angle BETWEEN the two sides?
- **Hint 2:** A right angle with hypotenuse → RHS.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — List the equal parts in order around the triangle. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-03-S1 -->

$$\text{∠B = ∠Q = 90°, AC = PR (hypotenuse), AB = PQ}$$

Step 2 — Match the pattern to a condition. <!-- MQ-G10C05-F-CONG_CONDI-03-S2 -->

$$\Rightarrow \text{RHS}$$

**Final answer:** $RHS (செ.ப.ப)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using AAA — equal angles alone do not prove congruence.

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C05", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-CONG", "diagram": null, "kind": "concept", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S2", "MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Congruence of Triangles · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-CONG`

**Question:** ΔABC ≡ ΔPQR. AB = 7 cm, BC = 10 cm and ∠ABC = 50°. Find PQ, QR and ∠PQR.

- **Hint 1:** The order of letters tells you which parts match.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Match vertices in order: A↔P, B↔Q, C↔R. <!-- MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S1 -->

$$A\leftrightarrow P,\ B\leftrightarrow Q,\ C\leftrightarrow R$$

Step 2 — Corresponding sides are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S2 -->

$$PQ = AB = 7\ \text{cm},\quad QR = BC = 10\ \text{cm}$$

Step 3 — Corresponding angles are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C05-G-CONG_FIND_-01-S3 -->

$$\angle PQR = \angle ABC = 50^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $PQ=7\text{ cm},\ QR=10\text{ cm},\ \angle PQR=50^\circ$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Matching vertices by position in the diagram instead of by the letter order.

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C05", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-CONG", "diagram": "GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S1", "MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S2", "MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S3", "MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S4", "MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Congruence of Triangles · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-CONG`

**Question:** In the figure, AB = AC and AD bisects ∠BAC, meeting BC at D. Prove that ΔABD ≡ ΔACD and hence that BD = DC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for a side shared by both triangles.
- **Hint 2:** The angle must be between the two sides for SAS.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Side: AB = AC. <!-- MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S1 -->

$$AB = AC\quad(\text{given / தரவு})$$

Step 2 — Angle: ∠BAD = ∠CAD. <!-- MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S2 -->

$$\angle BAD = \angle CAD\quad(\text{AD bisects }\angle A)$$

Step 3 — Side: AD is common. <!-- MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S3 -->

$$AD = AD\quad(\text{common / பொது})$$

Step 4 — State the congruence. <!-- MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S4 -->

$$\therefore \triangle ABD \equiv \triangle ACD\quad(\text{SAS})$$

Step 5 — Corresponding sides are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C05-A-CONG_PROOF-01-S5 -->

$$\therefore BD = DC$$

**Final answer:** $\triangle ABD\equiv\triangle ACD\ (\text{SAS}),\ BD=DC$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing statements without reasons — each statement needs a reason.

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

## G10C06 — Area

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C06", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MEN-SECTA", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SECTA`

**Question:** Find the area of a sector of radius 14 cm with centre angle 60°. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Area of the full circle is πr².
- **Hint 2:** The sector is 60/360 of the circle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the formula. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S1 -->

$$A = \pi r^2\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S2 -->

$$A = \frac{22}{7}\times 14\times 14\times\frac{60}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-01-S3 -->

$$A = \frac{308}{3}\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{308}{3}\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{308}{3}\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using 2πr (length) instead of πr² (area).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C06", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MEN-SECTA", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S1", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S2", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SECTA`

**Question:** Find the area of a sector of radius 21 cm with centre angle 90°. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Area of the full circle is πr².
- **Hint 2:** The sector is 90/360 of the circle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the formula. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S1 -->

$$A = \pi r^2\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S2 -->

$$A = \frac{22}{7}\times 21\times 21\times\frac{90}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-02-S3 -->

$$A = \frac{693}{2}\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{693}{2}\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{693}{2}\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using 2πr (length) instead of πr² (area).

$$A = \pi r^2\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-03-S2 -->

$$A = \frac{22}{7}\times 21\times 21\times\frac{60}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-03-S3 -->

$$A = 231\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $231\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $231\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using 2πr (length) instead of πr² (area).

#### MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C06", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MEN-SECTA", "diagram": "GEO-SECTOR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S1", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S2", "MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SECTA`

**Question:** Find the area of a sector of radius 21 cm with centre angle 45°. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-SECTOR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SECTOR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Area of the full circle is πr².
- **Hint 2:** The sector is 45/360 of the circle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the formula. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S1 -->

$$A = \pi r^2\times\frac{\theta}{360}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S2 -->

$$A = \frac{22}{7}\times 21\times 21\times\frac{45}{360}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C06-F-SECTOR_ARE-04-S3 -->

$$A = \frac{693}{4}\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{693}{4}\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{693}{4}\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using 2πr (length) instead of πr² (area).

**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-MEN-TRAP`

**Question:** A lamina is made of a trapezium with parallel sides 10 cm and 14 cm and perpendicular height 7 cm, joined to a semicircle of radius 7 cm. Find the total area. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `GEO-TRAP-SEMI-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TRAP-SEMI-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Split the lamina into shapes you know.
- **Hint 2:** Trapezium: ½(sum of parallel sides) × height.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Area of the trapezium. <!-- MQ-G10C06-G-TRAPEZIUM_-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{1}{2}(10+14)\times 7 = 84\ \text{cm}^2$$

Step 2 — Area of the semicircle. <!-- MQ-G10C06-G-TRAPEZIUM_-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{22}{7}\times 7^2 = 77\ \text{cm}^2$$

Step 3 — Add the two areas. <!-- MQ-G10C06-G-TRAPEZIUM_-01-S3 -->

$$84 + 77 = 161\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $161\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $161\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Forgetting the ½ in the semicircle area.

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C06", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-SECTA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SECTA`

**Question:** The area of a sector with centre angle 40° is 154 cm². Find the radius. (π = 22/7)

- **Hint 1:** Rearrange to get r² alone.
- **Hint 2:** Multiply by the reciprocals 7/22 and 360/θ.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the formula with r unknown. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S1 -->

$$154 = \frac{22}{7}\times r^2\times\frac{40}{360}$$

Step 2 — Make r² the subject. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S2 -->

$$r^2 = 154\times\frac{7}{22}\times\frac{360}{40} = 441$$

Step 3 — Take the square root. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-01-S3 -->

$$r = \sqrt{441} = 21\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $21\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $21\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Stopping at r² and forgetting the square root.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C06", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-SECTA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S1", "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S2", "MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Area · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SECTA`

**Question:** The area of a sector with centre angle 90° is \frac{77}{2} cm². Find the radius. (π = 22/7)

- **Hint 1:** Rearrange to get r² alone.
- **Hint 2:** Multiply by the reciprocals 7/22 and 360/θ.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the formula with r unknown. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S1 -->

$$\frac{77}{2} = \frac{22}{7}\times r^2\times\frac{90}{360}$$

Step 2 — Make r² the subject. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S2 -->

$$r^2 = \frac{77}{2}\times\frac{7}{22}\times\frac{360}{90} = 49$$

Step 3 — Take the square root. <!-- MQ-G10C06-A-RADIUS_FRO-03-S3 -->

$$r = \sqrt{49} = 7\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $7\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $7\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Stopping at r² and forgetting the square root.

## G10C07 — Factors of Quadratic Expressions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S1", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S2", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S3", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $x^{2} + 3x - 10$

- **Hint 1:** Product = constant term, sum = coefficient of x.
- **Hint 2:** Try factor pairs of the constant.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find two numbers whose product is -10 and sum is 3. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S1 -->

$$-2\times 5 = -10,\quad -2 + (5) = 3$$

Step 2 — Split the middle term. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S2 -->

$$x^2  - 2x  + 5x  - 10$$

Step 3 — Group and take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S3 -->

$$x(x  - 2)  + 5(x  - 2)$$

Step 4 — Final factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-01-S4 -->

$$(x  - 2)(x  + 5)$$

**Final answer:** $(x  - 2)(x  + 5)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Choosing numbers with the right product but wrong sign.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S1", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S2", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S3", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $x^{2} + 6x + 5$

- **Hint 1:** Product = constant term, sum = coefficient of x.
- **Hint 2:** Try factor pairs of the constant.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find two numbers whose product is 5 and sum is 6. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S1 -->

$$1\times 5 = 5,\quad 1 + (5) = 6$$

Step 2 — Split the middle term. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S2 -->

$$x^2  + 1x  + 5x  + 5$$

Step 3 — Group and take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S3 -->

$$x(x  + 1)  + 5(x  + 1)$$

Step 4 — Final factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-02-S4 -->

$$(x  + 1)(x  + 5)$$

**Final answer:** $(x  + 1)(x  + 5)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Choosing numbers with the right product but wrong sign.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S1", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S2", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S3", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $x^{2} - x - 12$

- **Hint 1:** Product = constant term, sum = coefficient of x.
- **Hint 2:** Try factor pairs of the constant.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find two numbers whose product is -12 and sum is -1. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S1 -->

$$3\times -4 = -12,\quad 3 + (-4) = -1$$

Step 2 — Split the middle term. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S2 -->

$$x^2  + 3x  - 4x  - 12$$

Step 3 — Group and take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S3 -->

$$x(x  + 3)  - 4(x  + 3)$$

Step 4 — Final factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-03-S4 -->

$$(x  + 3)(x  - 4)$$

**Final answer:** $(x  + 3)(x  - 4)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Choosing numbers with the right product but wrong sign.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S1", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S2", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S3", "MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $x^{2} + 8x + 12$

- **Hint 1:** Product = constant term, sum = coefficient of x.
- **Hint 2:** Try factor pairs of the constant.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find two numbers whose product is 12 and sum is 8. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S1 -->

$$2\times 6 = 12,\quad 2 + (6) = 8$$

Step 2 — Split the middle term. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S2 -->

$$x^2  + 2x  + 6x  + 12$$

Step 3 — Group and take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S3 -->

$$x(x  + 2)  + 6(x  + 2)$$

Step 4 — Final factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-F-FACTOR_MON-04-S4 -->

$$(x  + 2)(x  + 6)$$

**Final answer:** $(x  + 2)(x  + 6)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Choosing numbers with the right product but wrong sign.

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S1", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S2", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S3", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $6x^{2} - 13x - 5$

- **Hint 1:** Use the 'ac method': multiply a and c.
- **Hint 2:** Check by expanding your answer.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply the first and last coefficients: 6 × -5 = -30. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S1 -->

$$6\times(-5) = -30$$

Step 2 — Find two numbers with product -30 and sum -13. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S2 -->

$$-15\times(2) = -30,\quad -15+(2) = -13$$

Step 3 — Split the middle term and group. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S3 -->

$$6x^2  - 15x  + 2x  - 5$$

Step 4 — Take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-01-S4 -->

$$= (3x + 1)(2x - 5)$$

**Final answer:** $(3x + 1)(2x - 5)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Not checking by expanding — one sign error changes everything.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S1", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S2", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S3", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $2x^{2} + 7x + 3$

- **Hint 1:** Use the 'ac method': multiply a and c.
- **Hint 2:** Check by expanding your answer.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply the first and last coefficients: 2 × 3 = 6. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S1 -->

$$2\times(3) = 6$$

Step 2 — Find two numbers with product 6 and sum 7. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S2 -->

$$6\times(1) = 6,\quad 6+(1) = 7$$

Step 3 — Split the middle term and group. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S3 -->

$$2x^2  + 6x  + 1x  + 3$$

Step 4 — Take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-02-S4 -->

$$= (2x + 1)(x + 3)$$

**Final answer:** $(2x + 1)(x + 3)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Not checking by expanding — one sign error changes everything.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S1", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S2", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S3", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $3x^{2} - x - 2$

- **Hint 1:** Use the 'ac method': multiply a and c.
- **Hint 2:** Check by expanding your answer.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply the first and last coefficients: 3 × -2 = -6. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S1 -->

$$3\times(-2) = -6$$

Step 2 — Find two numbers with product -6 and sum -1. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S2 -->

$$-3\times(2) = -6,\quad -3+(2) = -1$$

Step 3 — Split the middle term and group. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S3 -->

$$3x^2  - 3x  + 2x  - 2$$

Step 4 — Take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-03-S4 -->

$$= (3x + 2)(x - 1)$$

**Final answer:** $(3x + 2)(x - 1)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Not checking by expanding — one sign error changes everything.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-FACT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S1", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S2", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S3", "MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-FACT`

**Question:** Factorise: $2x^{2} + x - 10$

- **Hint 1:** Use the 'ac method': multiply a and c.
- **Hint 2:** Check by expanding your answer.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply the first and last coefficients: 2 × -10 = -20. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S1 -->

$$2\times(-10) = -20$$

Step 2 — Find two numbers with product -20 and sum 1. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S2 -->

$$-4\times(5) = -20,\quad -4+(5) = 1$$

Step 3 — Split the middle term and group. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S3 -->

$$2x^2  - 4x  + 5x  - 10$$

Step 4 — Take common factors. <!-- MQ-G10C07-G-FACTOR_NON-04-S4 -->

$$= (2x + 5)(x - 2)$$

**Final answer:** $(2x + 5)(x - 2)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Not checking by expanding — one sign error changes everything.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-DOS", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S1", "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S2", "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Factors of Quadratic Expressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-DOS`

**Question:** Factorise completely: $5x^2 - 245$

- **Hint 1:** Always look for a common factor first.
- **Hint 2:** Is the bracket a² − b²?

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Take out the common factor. <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S1 -->

$$5x^2 - 245 = 5(x^2 - 49)$$

Step 2 — Recognise a difference of two squares. <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S2 -->

$$x^2 - 49 = x^2 - 7^2$$

Step 3 — Use a² − b² = (a + b)(a − b). <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-01-S3 -->

$$= 5(x+7)(x-7)$$

**Final answer:** $5(x+7)(x-7)$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Stopping at k(x² − a²) — 'completely' means factorise the bracket too.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-DOS", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S1", "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S2", "MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S3"]} -->

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Take out the common factor. <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S1 -->

$$3x^2 - 48 = 3(x^2 - 16)$$

Step 2 — Recognise a difference of two squares. <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S2 -->

$$x^2 - 16 = x^2 - 4^2$$

Step 3 — Use a² − b² = (a + b)(a − b). <!-- MQ-G10C07-A-FACTOR_DOS-02-S3 -->

$$= 3(x+4)(x-4)$$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Stopping at k(x² − a²) — 'completely' means factorise the bracket too.

## G10C08 — Triangles I

#### MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-TRISUM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S1", "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S2", "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-TRISUM`

**Question:** Two angles of a triangle are 48° and 67°. Find the third angle.

- **Hint 1:** Sum of angles in a triangle = 180°.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angles of a triangle add up to 180°. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S1 -->

$$x + 48 + 67 = 180$$

Step 2 — Add the known angles. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S2 -->

$$x + 115 = 180$$

Step 3 — Subtract. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-01-S3 -->

$$x = 180 - 115 = 65^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $65^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $65^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

#### MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-EXT", "diagram": "GEO-EXTANGLE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S1", "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-EXT`

**Question:** In ΔABC, BC is produced to D. ∠BAC = 55° and ∠ABC = 80°. Find ∠ACD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-EXTANGLE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-EXTANGLE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** ∠ACD is outside the triangle at C.
- **Hint 2:** Use the exterior angle theorem.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Exterior angle = sum of the two interior opposite angles. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ACD = \angle BAC + \angle ABC$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ACD = 55^\circ + 80^\circ = 135^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $135^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $135^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-TRISUM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S3", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-TRISUM`

**Question:** The angles of a triangle are $(2x + 10)^\circ$, $x^\circ$ and $95^\circ$. Find x and the largest angle.

- **Hint 1:** Angle sum = 180° gives an equation.
- **Hint 2:** After finding x, substitute back.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the angle-sum equation. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + x + 95 = 180$$

Step 2 — Collect terms. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S2 -->

$$3x + 105 = 180$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S3 -->

$$3x = 75 \Rightarrow x = 25$$

Step 4 — Find each angle and pick the largest. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S4 -->

$$2x+10 = 60^\circ,\ x = 25^\circ,\ 95^\circ \Rightarrow \max = 95^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=25,\ \text{largest}=95^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=25,\ \text{largest}=95^\circ$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Giving x as the final answer when the largest angle is asked.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-TRISUM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S1", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S2", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S3", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-TRISUM`

**Question:** The angles of a triangle are $(3x + 10)^\circ$, $x^\circ$ and $90^\circ$. Find x and the largest angle.

- **Hint 1:** Angle sum = 180° gives an equation.
- **Hint 2:** After finding x, substitute back.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the angle-sum equation. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S1 -->

$$(3x+10) + x + 90 = 180$$

Step 2 — Collect terms. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S2 -->

$$4x + 100 = 180$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S3 -->

$$4x = 80 \Rightarrow x = 20$$

Step 4 — Find each angle and pick the largest. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-02-S4 -->

$$3x+10 = 70^\circ,\ x = 20^\circ,\ 90^\circ \Rightarrow \max = 90^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=20,\ \text{largest}=90^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=20,\ \text{largest}=90^\circ$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Giving x as the final answer when the largest angle is asked.

## G10C09 — Triangles II

#### MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C09", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-ISOS", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S2", "MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles II · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-ISOS`

**Question:** In ΔABC, AB = AC and ∠BAC = 100°. Find ∠ABC.

- **Hint 1:** AB = AC means the base angles B and C are equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angles opposite equal sides are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ABC = \angle ACB = y$$

Step 2 — Use the angle sum. <!-- MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S2 -->

$$100 + y + y = 180 \Rightarrow 2y = 80$$

Step 3 — Divide by 2. <!-- MQ-G10C09-F-ISOSCELES_-01-S3 -->

$$y = 40^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $40^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $40^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C09", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-ISOS", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S2", "MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles II · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-ISOS`

**Question:** In ΔPQR, PQ = PR and ∠PQR = 72°. QR is produced to S. Find ∠QPR and ∠PRS.

- **Hint 1:** Which sides are equal? So which angles are equal?

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Base angles are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S1 -->

$$\angle PRQ = \angle PQR = 72^\circ$$

Step 2 — Apex angle from the angle sum. <!-- MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S2 -->

$$\angle QPR = 180 - 2\times72 = 36^\circ$$

Step 3 — Angles on a straight line add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G10C09-G-ISOSCELES_-01-S3 -->

$$\angle PRS = 180 - 72 = 108^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle QPR=36^\circ,\ \angle PRS=108^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle QPR=36^\circ,\ \angle PRS=108^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C09", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-ISOS", "diagram": "GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S2", "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S3", "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S4", "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S5", "MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S6"]} -->
**Topic:** Triangles II · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-ISOS`

**Question:** In ΔABC, AB = AC. D is the midpoint of BC. Prove that AD is perpendicular to BC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ISOS-BISECT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Prove two triangles congruent first.
- **Hint 2:** Two equal angles on a straight line are each 90°.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare ΔABD and ΔACD: AB = AC. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S1 -->

$$AB = AC\ (\text{given})$$

Step 2 — BD = DC. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S2 -->

$$BD = DC\ (D\text{ is the midpoint})$$

Step 3 — AD is common. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S3 -->

$$AD = AD\ (\text{common})$$

Step 4 — Congruent by SSS. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S4 -->

$$\triangle ABD\equiv\triangle ACD\ (\text{SSS})$$

Step 5 — So the angles at D are equal; they lie on a straight line. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S5 -->

$$\angle ADB = \angle ADC,\ \angle ADB + \angle ADC = 180^\circ$$

Step 6 — Each angle is 90°. <!-- MQ-G10C09-A-ISOSCELES_-01-S6 -->

$$\angle ADB = 90^\circ \Rightarrow AD\perp BC$$

**Final answer:** $AD\perp BC$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

## G10C10 — Inverse Proportions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C10", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-INV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01-S1", "MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Inverse Proportions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-INV`

**Question:** 12 workers can finish a job in 5 days. How many days will 15 workers take (working at the same rate)?

- **Hint 1:** More workers → fewer days (inverse proportion).
- **Hint 2:** Workers × days stays the same.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Find the total work in worker-days. <!-- MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01-S1 -->

$$12\times 5 = 60\ \text{worker-days}$$

Step 2 — Divide by the new number of workers. <!-- MQ-G10C10-F-MEN_DAYS-01-S2 -->

$$60\div 15 = 4\ \text{days}$$

**Final answer:** $4\ \text{days}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $4\ \text{days}$ · **Unit:** days

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using direct proportion (multiplying by m2/m1).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C10", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-NUM-INV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S1", "MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S2", "MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Inverse Proportions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-NUM-INV`

**Question:** 12 people can build a wall in 10 days. To finish it in 8 days, how many MORE people are needed?

- **Hint 1:** First find how many people are needed in total.
- **Hint 2:** 'More' means subtract the people already there.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total work. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S1 -->

$$12\times10 = 120\ \text{person-days}$$

Step 2 — People needed for the new time. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S2 -->

$$120\div8 = 15$$

Step 3 — Extra people. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-01-S3 -->

$$15 - 12 = 3$$

**Final answer:** $3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

$$60\div4 = 15$$

**Question:** 6 people can build a wall in 15 days. To finish it in 10 days, how many MORE people are needed?

- **Hint 1:** First find how many people are needed in total.
- **Hint 2:** 'More' means subtract the people already there.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total work. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-04-S1 -->

$$6\times15 = 90\ \text{person-days}$$

Step 2 — People needed for the new time. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-04-S2 -->

$$90\div10 = 9$$

Step 3 — Extra people. <!-- MQ-G10C10-G-EXTRA_WORK-04-S3 -->

$$9 - 6 = 3$$

**Final answer:** $3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C10", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-INV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S3", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Inverse Proportions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-INV`

**Question:** 10 workers planned to finish a job in 18 days. After 6 days, 2 workers left. How many more days will the remaining workers take to finish the job?

- **Hint 1:** Work in worker-days: done + remaining = total.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total work. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S1 -->

$$10\times18 = 180\ \text{worker-days}$$

Step 2 — Work already done. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S2 -->

$$10\times6 = 60$$

Step 3 — Work remaining. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S3 -->

$$180 - 60 = 120$$

Step 4 — Days for the remaining workers. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-01-S4 -->

$$120\div(10-2) = 120\div8 = 15$$

**Final answer:** $15\ \text{days}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $15\ \text{days}$ · **Unit:** days

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C10", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-INV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S1", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S2", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S3", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Inverse Proportions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-INV`

**Question:** 15 workers planned to finish a job in 12 days. After 4 days, 5 workers left. How many more days will the remaining workers take to finish the job?

- **Hint 1:** Work in worker-days: done + remaining = total.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total work. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S1 -->

$$15\times12 = 180\ \text{worker-days}$$

Step 2 — Work already done. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S2 -->

$$15\times4 = 60$$

Step 3 — Work remaining. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S3 -->

$$180 - 60 = 120$$

Step 4 — Days for the remaining workers. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-02-S4 -->

$$120\div(15-5) = 120\div10 = 12$$

**Final answer:** $12\ \text{days}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $12\ \text{days}$ · **Unit:** days

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C10", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-INV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S1", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S2", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S3", "MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Inverse Proportions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-INV`

**Question:** 12 workers planned to finish a job in 20 days. After 5 days, 3 workers left. How many more days will the remaining workers take to finish the job?

- **Hint 1:** Work in worker-days: done + remaining = total.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total work. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S1 -->

$$12\times20 = 240\ \text{worker-days}$$

Step 2 — Work already done. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S2 -->

$$12\times5 = 60$$

Step 3 — Work remaining. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S3 -->

$$240 - 60 = 180$$

Step 4 — Days for the remaining workers. <!-- MQ-G10C10-A-PARTIAL_WO-03-S4 -->

$$180\div(12-3) = 180\div9 = 20$$

**Final answer:** $20\ \text{days}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $20\ \text{days}$ · **Unit:** days

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C11 — Data Representation
