#### MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C11", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-STA-PIE", "diagram": "STA-PIE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-STA-PIE`

**Question:** In a survey of 360 students, 45 chose cricket. Find the angle of the cricket sector in a pie chart.

**Diagram:** `STA-PIE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Statistical_Charts/STA-PIE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The whole circle (360°) represents all students.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle = (part ÷ total) × 360°. <!-- MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{45}{360}\times 360^\circ$$

Step 2 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G10C11-F-PIE_ANGLE-01-S2 -->

$$= 45^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $45^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $45^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

#### MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C11", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-STA-PIE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01-S1", "MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-STA-PIE`

**Question:** A pie chart of 120 people has sectors of 60°, 150° and 60°, and one more sector for 'Other'. How many people are in 'Other'?

- **Hint 1:** Find the missing angle first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angles in a circle add to 360°. <!-- MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01-S1 -->

$$x = 360 - 60 - 150 - 60 = 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Convert the angle to people. <!-- MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{90}{360}\times120 = 30$$

**Final answer:** $30$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $30$

$$x = 360 - 60 - 150 - 60 = 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Convert the angle to people. <!-- MQ-G10C11-A-PIE_MISSIN-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{90}{360}\times240 = 60$$

**Final answer:** $60$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $60$

## G10C12 — Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $3a^2$ and $9ab$.

- **Hint 1:** Highest power of each letter.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — LCM of the numbers. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(3,9) = 9$$

Step 2 — For each letter take the highest power. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S2 -->

$$a^{2},\ b^{1}$$

Step 3 — Multiply together. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-01-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 9a^2b$$

**Final answer:** $9a^2b$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Taking the lowest power (that gives the HCF).

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S1", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S2", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $4x^2y$ and $6xy^2$.

- **Hint 1:** Highest power of each letter.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — LCM of the numbers. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(4,6) = 12$$

Step 2 — For each letter take the highest power. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S2 -->

$$x^{2},\ y^{2}$$

Step 3 — Multiply together. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-02-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 12x^2y^2$$

**Final answer:** $12x^2y^2$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Taking the lowest power (that gives the HCF).

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S1", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S2", "MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $8x$ and $12x^3$.

- **Hint 1:** Highest power of each letter.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — LCM of the numbers. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(8,12) = 24$$

Step 2 — For each letter take the highest power. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S2 -->

$$x^{3}$$

Step 3 — Multiply together. <!-- MQ-G10C12-F-LCM_MONO-03-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 24x^3$$

**Final answer:** $24x^3$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Taking the lowest power (that gives the HCF).

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S1", "MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S2", "MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 - 25$ and $x^2 + 5x$.

- **Hint 1:** Factorise both expressions completely first.
- **Hint 2:** A common factor is written only once.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the first expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S1 -->

$$x^2-25 = (x+5)(x-5)$$

Step 2 — Factorise the second expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S2 -->

$$x^2+5x = x(x+5)$$

Step 3 — Take every factor once (highest power). <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-01-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = x(x+5)(x-5)$$

**Final answer:** $x(x+5)(x-5)$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-02

**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 - 4$ and $x^2 + 2x$.

- **Hint 1:** Factorise both expressions completely first.
- **Hint 2:** A common factor is written only once.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the first expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-02-S1 -->

$$x^2-4 = (x+2)(x-2)$$

Step 2 — Factorise the second expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-02-S2 -->

$$x^2+2x = x(x+2)$$

Step 3 — Take every factor once (highest power). <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-02-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = x(x+2)(x-2)$$

**Final answer:** $x(x+2)(x-2)$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 - 16$ and $x^2 + 4x$.

- **Hint 1:** Factorise both expressions completely first.
- **Hint 2:** A common factor is written only once.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the first expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-03-S1 -->

$$x^2-16 = (x+4)(x-4)$$

Step 2 — Factorise the second expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-03-S2 -->

$$x^2+4x = x(x+4)$$

Step 3 — Take every factor once (highest power). <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-03-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = x(x+4)(x-4)$$

**Final answer:** $x(x+4)(x-4)$

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 - 9$ and $x^2 + 3x$.

- **Hint 1:** Factorise both expressions completely first.
- **Hint 2:** A common factor is written only once.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the first expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-04-S1 -->

$$x^2-9 = (x+3)(x-3)$$

Step 2 — Factorise the second expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-04-S2 -->

$$x^2+3x = x(x+3)$$

Step 3 — Take every factor once (highest power). <!-- MQ-G10C12-G-LCM_BINOM-04-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = x(x+3)(x-3)$$

**Final answer:** $x(x+3)(x-3)$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 + 6x + 9$, $x^2 - 9$ and $2x + 6$.

- **Hint 1:** (x + a)² appears in the first one — so the LCM needs the square.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise each expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S1 -->

$$(x+3)^2,\quad (x+3)(x-3),\quad 2(x+3)$$

Step 2 — Highest power of each factor. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S2 -->

$$2,\ (x+3)^2,\ (x-3)$$

Step 3 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-01-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 2(x+3)^2(x-3)$$

**Final answer:** $2(x+3)^2(x-3)$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S1", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S2", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 + 2x + 1$, $x^2 - 1$ and $2x + 2$.

- **Hint 1:** (x + a)² appears in the first one — so the LCM needs the square.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise each expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S1 -->

$$(x+1)^2,\quad (x+1)(x-1),\quad 2(x+1)$$

Step 2 — Highest power of each factor. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S2 -->

$$2,\ (x+1)^2,\ (x-1)$$

Step 3 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-02-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 2(x+1)^2(x-1)$$

**Final answer:** $2(x+1)^2(x-1)$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C12", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-LCM", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S1", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S2", "MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Least Common Multiple of Algebraic Expressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LCM`

**Question:** Find the LCM of $x^2 + 4x + 4$, $x^2 - 4$ and $2x + 4$.

- **Hint 1:** (x + a)² appears in the first one — so the LCM needs the square.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise each expression. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S1 -->

$$(x+2)^2,\quad (x+2)(x-2),\quad 2(x+2)$$

Step 2 — Highest power of each factor. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S2 -->

$$2,\ (x+2)^2,\ (x-2)$$

Step 3 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C12-A-LCM_THREE-03-S3 -->

$$\text{LCM} = 2(x+2)^2(x-2)$$

**Final answer:** $2(x+2)^2(x-2)$

#### MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{3x}{7} + \frac{4x}{7}$

- **Hint 1:** Same denominator → add the tops.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Denominators are equal, so add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{3x+4x}{7}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C13-F-AF_SAME_DE-01-S2 -->

$$= \frac{7x}{7}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{7x}{7}$

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S3", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x}{2} + \frac{x+1}{3}$

- **Hint 1:** Multiply the whole numerator (x + 1) — use brackets.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(2,3) = 6$$

Step 2 — Rewrite each fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{3x}{6} + \frac{2(x+1)}{6}$$

Step 3 — Expand and add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S3 -->

$$= \frac{3x + 2x + 2}{6}$$

Step 4 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-01-S4 -->

$$= \frac{5x + 2}{6}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{5x+2}{6}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only x and not the 1 in (x + 1).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S1", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S2", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S3", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x}{2} + \frac{x+1}{5}$

- **Hint 1:** Multiply the whole numerator (x + 1) — use brackets.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(2,5) = 10$$

Step 2 — Rewrite each fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{5x}{10} + \frac{2(x+1)}{10}$$

Step 3 — Expand and add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S3 -->

$$= \frac{5x + 2x + 2}{10}$$

Step 4 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-02-S4 -->

$$= \frac{7x + 2}{10}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{7x+2}{10}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only x and not the 1 in (x + 1).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S1", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S2", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S3", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x}{3} + \frac{x+1}{4}$

- **Hint 1:** Multiply the whole numerator (x + 1) — use brackets.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(3,4) = 12$$

Step 2 — Rewrite each fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S2 -->

$$\frac{4x}{12} + \frac{3(x+1)}{12}$$

Step 3 — Expand and add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S3 -->

$$= \frac{4x + 3x + 3}{12}$$

Step 4 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-03-S4 -->

$$= \frac{7x + 3}{12}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{7x+3}{12}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only x and not the 1 in (x + 1).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S1", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S2", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S3", "MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x}{4} + \frac{x+1}{5}$

- **Hint 1:** Multiply the whole numerator (x + 1) — use brackets.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S1 -->

$$\text{LCM}(4,5) = 20$$

Step 2 — Rewrite each fraction. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S2 -->

$$\frac{5x}{20} + \frac{4(x+1)}{20}$$

Step 3 — Expand and add the numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S3 -->

$$= \frac{5x + 4x + 4}{20}$$

Step 4 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C13-G-AF_DIFF_DE-04-S4 -->

$$= \frac{9x + 4}{20}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{9x+4}{20}$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only x and not the 1 in (x + 1).

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S1", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S2", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S3", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{2}{x-4} - \frac{8}{x^2-4x}$

- **Hint 1:** Factorise denominators before finding the LCM.
- **Hint 2:** After subtracting, check if the top factorises.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the second denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S1 -->

$$x^2-4x = x(x-4)$$

Step 2 — Common denominator x(x − 4). <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{2x}{x(x-4)} - \frac{8}{x(x-4)}$$

Step 3 — Subtract numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S3 -->

$$= \frac{2x-8}{x(x-4)}$$

Step 4 — Factorise and cancel. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-01-S4 -->

$$= \frac{2(x-4)}{x(x-4)} = \frac{2}{x}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{2}{x}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S1", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S2", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S3", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{2}{x-1} - \frac{2}{x^2-1x}$

- **Hint 1:** Factorise denominators before finding the LCM.
- **Hint 2:** After subtracting, check if the top factorises.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the second denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S1 -->

$$x^2-1x = x(x-1)$$

Step 2 — Common denominator x(x − 1). <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{2x}{x(x-1)} - \frac{2}{x(x-1)}$$

Step 3 — Subtract numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S3 -->

$$= \frac{2x-2}{x(x-1)}$$

Step 4 — Factorise and cancel. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-02-S4 -->

$$= \frac{2(x-1)}{x(x-1)} = \frac{2}{x}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{2}{x}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S1", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S2", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S3", "MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{2}{x-2} - \frac{4}{x^2-2x}$

- **Hint 1:** Factorise denominators before finding the LCM.
- **Hint 2:** After subtracting, check if the top factorises.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the second denominator. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S1 -->

$$x^2-2x = x(x-2)$$

Step 2 — Common denominator x(x − 2). <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S2 -->

$$\frac{2x}{x(x-2)} - \frac{4}{x(x-2)}$$

Step 3 — Subtract numerators. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S3 -->

$$= \frac{2x-4}{x(x-2)}$$

Step 4 — Factorise and cancel. <!-- MQ-G10C13-A-AF_FACTOR-03-S4 -->

$$= \frac{2(x-2)}{x(x-2)} = \frac{2}{x}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{2}{x}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C14 — Percentages

#### MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C14", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-PROFIT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S1", "MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S2", "MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentages · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-PROFIT`

**Question:** A trader buys an item for Rs. 1500 and sells it for Rs. 1650. Find the profit percentage.

- **Hint 1:** Profit % is always on the COST price.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Profit = selling price − cost price. <!-- MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S1 -->

$$1650 - 1500 = 150$$

Step 2 — Profit % = profit ÷ cost price × 100. <!-- MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{150}{1500}\times 100$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G10C14-F-PROFIT_PER-01-S3 -->

$$= 10\%$$

**Final answer:** $10\%$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $10\%$ · **Unit:** %

$$7500 - 1125 = 6375$$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C14", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-SI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S1", "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S2", "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentages · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SI`

**Question:** Rs. 25 000 was deposited at simple interest. After 4 years the total amount was Rs. 37 000. Find the annual interest rate.

- **Hint 1:** Find the interest first.
- **Hint 2:** I = PRT/100 ⇒ R = 100I/(PT).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest = amount − principal. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S1 -->

$$I = 37\,000 - 25\,000 = 12\,000$$

Step 2 — Interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S2 -->

$$12\,000\div 4 = 3000$$

Step 3 — Rate = yearly interest ÷ principal × 100. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{3000}{25\,000}\times 100 = 12\%$$

**Final answer:** $12\%$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $12\%$ · **Unit:** %

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using the total amount as the interest.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C14", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-SI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S1", "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S2", "MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentages · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SI`

**Question:** Rs. 20 000 was deposited at simple interest. After 3 years the total amount was Rs. 26 000. Find the annual interest rate.

- **Hint 1:** Find the interest first.
- **Hint 2:** I = PRT/100 ⇒ R = 100I/(PT).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest = amount − principal. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S1 -->

$$I = 26\,000 - 20\,000 = 6000$$

Step 2 — Interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S2 -->

$$6000\div 3 = 2000$$

Step 3 — Rate = yearly interest ÷ principal × 100. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-02-S3 -->

$$\frac{2000}{20\,000}\times 100 = 10\%$$

**Final answer:** $10\%$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $10\%$ · **Unit:** %

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Using the total amount as the interest.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Question:** Rs. 50 000 was deposited at simple interest. After 4 years the total amount was Rs. 74 000. Find the annual interest rate.

- **Hint 1:** Find the interest first.
- **Hint 2:** I = PRT/100 ⇒ R = 100I/(PT).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest = amount − principal. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-03-S1 -->

$$I = 74\,000 - 50\,000 = 24\,000$$

Step 2 — Interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-03-S2 -->

$$24\,000\div 4 = 6000$$

Step 3 — Rate = yearly interest ÷ principal × 100. <!-- MQ-G10C14-A-SIMPLE_INT-03-S3 -->

$$\frac{6000}{50\,000}\times 100 = 12\%$$

#### MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-LIN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S1", "MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S2", "MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-LIN`

**Question:** Solve: $\frac{x + 5}{2} = 5$

- **Hint 1:** Remove the fraction first by multiplying.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply both sides by 2. <!-- MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S1 -->

$$x + 5 = 5\times 2 = 10$$

Step 2 — Subtract 5 from both sides. <!-- MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S2 -->

$$x = 10 - 5$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G10C15-F-LINEAR_FRA-01-S3 -->

$$x = 5$$

**Final answer:** $x = 5$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 5$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SIM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S3", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S4", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SIM`

**Question:** Solve the simultaneous equations: $3x  + 2y = 7$ and $2x  - 3y = 9$

- **Hint 1:** Make one variable's coefficients equal with opposite signs, then add.
- **Hint 2:** Always check in the other equation.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Make the y-coefficients equal: multiply (1) by 3 and (2) by 2. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S1 -->

$$9x  + 6y = 21\quad(3),\qquad 4x  - 6y = 18\quad(4)$$

Step 2 — Add (3) and (4) to remove y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S2 -->

$$13x = 39$$

Step 3 — Find x. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S3 -->

$$x = 3$$

Step 4 — Substitute x in (1) to find y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S4 -->

$$3(3)  + 2y = 7 \Rightarrow y = -1$$

Step 5 — Check in (2). <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-01-S5 -->

$$2(3)  - 3(-1) = 9\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x = 3,\ y = -1$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 3,\ y = -1$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only the left side of an equation.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SIM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S1", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S2", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S3", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S4", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SIM`

**Question:** Solve the simultaneous equations: $x  + 2y = 9$ and $3x  - 1y = 13$

- **Hint 1:** Make one variable's coefficients equal with opposite signs, then add.
- **Hint 2:** Always check in the other equation.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Make the y-coefficients equal: multiply (1) by 1 and (2) by 2. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S1 -->

$$1x  + 2y = 9\quad(3),\qquad 6x  - 2y = 26\quad(4)$$

Step 2 — Add (3) and (4) to remove y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S2 -->

$$7x = 35$$

Step 3 — Find x. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S3 -->

$$x = 5$$

Step 4 — Substitute x in (1) to find y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S4 -->

$$1(5)  + 2y = 9 \Rightarrow y = 2$$

Step 5 — Check in (2). <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-02-S5 -->

$$3(5)  - 1(2) = 13\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x = 5,\ y = 2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 5,\ y = 2$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only the left side of an equation.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SIM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S1", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S2", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S3", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S4", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SIM`

**Question:** Solve the simultaneous equations: $3x  + 2y = 12$ and $2x  - 3y = -5$

- **Hint 1:** Make one variable's coefficients equal with opposite signs, then add.
- **Hint 2:** Always check in the other equation.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Make the y-coefficients equal: multiply (1) by 3 and (2) by 2. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S1 -->

$$9x  + 6y = 36\quad(3),\qquad 4x  - 6y = -10\quad(4)$$

Step 2 — Add (3) and (4) to remove y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S2 -->

$$13x = 26$$

Step 3 — Find x. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S3 -->

$$x = 2$$

Step 4 — Substitute x in (1) to find y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S4 -->

$$3(2)  + 2y = 12 \Rightarrow y = 3$$

Step 5 — Check in (2). <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-03-S5 -->

$$2(2)  - 3(3) = -5\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x = 2,\ y = 3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 2,\ y = 3$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only the left side of an equation.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SIM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S1", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S2", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S3", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S4", "MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SIM`

**Question:** Solve the simultaneous equations: $2x  + 3y = 13$ and $3x  - 2y = 0$

- **Hint 1:** Make one variable's coefficients equal with opposite signs, then add.
- **Hint 2:** Always check in the other equation.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Make the y-coefficients equal: multiply (1) by 2 and (2) by 3. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S1 -->

$$4x  + 6y = 26\quad(3),\qquad 9x  - 6y = 0\quad(4)$$

Step 2 — Add (3) and (4) to remove y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S2 -->

$$13x = 26$$

Step 3 — Find x. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S3 -->

$$x = 2$$

Step 4 — Substitute x in (1) to find y. <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S4 -->

$$2(2)  + 3y = 13 \Rightarrow y = 3$$

Step 5 — Check in (2). <!-- MQ-G10C15-G-SIMULTANEO-04-S5 -->

$$3(2)  - 2(3) = 0\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x = 2,\ y = 3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 2,\ y = 3$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying only the left side of an equation.

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C15", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3", "MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S4", "MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** The length of a rectangle is 4 m more than its width. Its area is 77 m². Form an equation in x (width) and find the width.

- **Hint 1:** Let width = x, then length = x + d.
- **Hint 2:** Bring all terms to one side and factorise.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write length in terms of x. <!-- MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1 -->

$$\text{length} = x + 4$$

Step 2 — Area = length × width. <!-- MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2 -->

$$x(x+4) = 77$$

Step 3 — Rearrange to = 0. <!-- MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3 -->

$$x^2 + 4x - 77 = 0$$

Step 4 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S4 -->

$$(x - 7)(x + 11) = 0$$

Step 5 — Solve and reject the negative value (a length cannot be negative). <!-- MQ-G10C15-A-QUAD_FACTO-01-S5 -->

$$x = 7\ \text{or}\ x = -11 \Rightarrow x = 7\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $7\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $7\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Giving the negative root as an answer for a length.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C16 — Parallelograms I

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": "GEO-PARA-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** In parallelogram ABCD, ∠DAB = 75°. Find ∠ABC, ∠BCD and ∠CDA.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Opposite angles equal; neighbouring angles add to 180°.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Adjacent angles of a parallelogram add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ABC = 180 - 75 = 105^\circ$$

Step 2 — Opposite angles are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C16-F-PARA_ANGLE-01-S2 -->

$$\angle BCD = \angle DAB = 75^\circ,\quad \angle CDA = \angle ABC = 105^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $105^\circ,\ 75^\circ,\ 105^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $105^\circ,\ 75^\circ,\ 105^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

$$\angle ABC = 180 - 62 = 118^\circ$$

#### MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** The diagonals of parallelogram ABCD meet at O. AO = $(2x + 1)$ cm and OC = $(x + 3)$ cm. Find x and the length of AC.

- **Hint 1:** O is the midpoint of AC.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonals of a parallelogram bisect each other. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S1 -->

$$AO = OC \Rightarrow 2x + 1 = x + 3$$

Step 2 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S2 -->

$$1x = 2 \Rightarrow x = 2$$

Step 3 — Find AO, then AC = 2 × AO. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-01-S3 -->

$$AO = 5,\quad AC = 2\times5 = 10\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $x=2,\ AC=10\text{ cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=2,\ AC=10\text{ cm}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S1", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S2", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** The diagonals of parallelogram ABCD meet at O. AO = $(3x + 1)$ cm and OC = $(x + 5)$ cm. Find x and the length of AC.

- **Hint 1:** O is the midpoint of AC.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonals of a parallelogram bisect each other. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S1 -->

$$AO = OC \Rightarrow 3x + 1 = x + 5$$

Step 2 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S2 -->

$$2x = 4 \Rightarrow x = 2$$

Step 3 — Find AO, then AC = 2 × AO. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-02-S3 -->

$$AO = 7,\quad AC = 2\times7 = 14\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $x=2,\ AC=14\text{ cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=2,\ AC=14\text{ cm}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S1", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S2", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** The diagonals of parallelogram ABCD meet at O. AO = $(2x + 1)$ cm and OC = $(x + 6)$ cm. Find x and the length of AC.

- **Hint 1:** O is the midpoint of AC.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonals of a parallelogram bisect each other. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S1 -->

$$AO = OC \Rightarrow 2x + 1 = x + 6$$

Step 2 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S2 -->

$$1x = 5 \Rightarrow x = 5$$

Step 3 — Find AO, then AC = 2 × AO. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-03-S3 -->

$$AO = 11,\quad AC = 2\times11 = 22\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $x=5,\ AC=22\text{ cm}$

#### MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S1", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S2", "MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** The diagonals of parallelogram ABCD meet at O. AO = $(4x + 1)$ cm and OC = $(x + 13)$ cm. Find x and the length of AC.

- **Hint 1:** O is the midpoint of AC.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonals of a parallelogram bisect each other. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S1 -->

$$AO = OC \Rightarrow 4x + 1 = x + 13$$

Step 2 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S2 -->

$$3x = 12 \Rightarrow x = 4$$

Step 3 — Find AO, then AC = 2 × AO. <!-- MQ-G10C16-G-PARA_DIAGO-04-S3 -->

$$AO = 17,\quad AC = 2\times17 = 34\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $x=4,\ AC=34\text{ cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=4,\ AC=34\text{ cm}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C16", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-PARA", "diagram": "GEO-PARA-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S1", "MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S2", "MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S3", "MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S4", "MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms I · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARA`

**Question:** ABCD is a parallelogram. E and F are points on AB and DC such that AE = CF. Prove that DE = BF.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Choose two triangles that contain DE and BF.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare ΔADE and ΔCBF: AD = CB. <!-- MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S1 -->

$$AD = CB\ (\text{opposite sides of a parallelogram})$$

Step 2 — ∠DAE = ∠BCF. <!-- MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S2 -->

$$\angle DAE = \angle BCF\ (\text{opposite angles of a parallelogram})$$

Step 3 — AE = CF. <!-- MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S3 -->

$$AE = CF\ (\text{given})$$

Step 4 — Congruent by SAS. <!-- MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S4 -->

$$\triangle ADE\equiv\triangle CBF\ (\text{SAS})$$

Step 5 — Corresponding sides. <!-- MQ-G10C16-A-PARA_PROOF-01-S5 -->

$$\therefore DE = BF$$

**Final answer:** $DE = BF$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PARACOND", "diagram": null, "kind": "concept", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01-S1", "MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms II · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARACOND`

**Question:** In quadrilateral ABCD, one pair of opposite sides is equal and parallel. What type of quadrilateral is ABCD? Give the reason.

- **Hint 1:** Any ONE of the four conditions is enough.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Recall the conditions for a parallelogram. <!-- MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01-S1 -->

$$\text{4 conditions (sides, angles, diagonals, one pair equal \& parallel)}$$

Step 2 — The given fact is one of them. <!-- MQ-G10C17-F-PARA_CONDI-01-S2 -->

$$\Rightarrow ABCD\ \text{is a parallelogram}$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Parallelogram}$

#### MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PARACOND", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms II · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARACOND`

**Question:** In quadrilateral PQRS, PQ = $(2x+1)$ cm, SR = $(x+5)$ cm, PS = $(3y)$ cm and QR = $(2y+3)$ cm. Find the values of x and y for which PQRS is a parallelogram (opposite sides equal).

- **Hint 1:** Set each pair of opposite sides equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite sides equal: PQ = SR. <!-- MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01-S1 -->

$$2x + 1 = x + 5 \Rightarrow x = 4$$

Step 2 — PS = QR. <!-- MQ-G10C17-G-PARA_FIND_-01-S2 -->

$$3y = 2y + 3 \Rightarrow y = 3$$

**Final answer:** $x=4,\ y=3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=4,\ y=3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-PARACOND", "diagram": "GEO-PARA-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S1", "MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S2", "MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S3", "MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Parallelograms II · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PARACOND`

**Question:** In parallelogram ABCD, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and DC. Prove that APCQ is a parallelogram.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Use the condition 'one pair of sides equal and parallel'.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — AB = DC and AB ∥ DC. <!-- MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S1 -->

$$AB = DC,\ AB\parallel DC\ (\text{ABCD is a parallelogram})$$

Step 2 — Half of equal sides are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S2 -->

$$AP = \tfrac12 AB = \tfrac12 DC = QC$$

Step 3 — AP lies on AB and QC lies on DC. <!-- MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S3 -->

$$AP\parallel QC$$

Step 4 — One pair of opposite sides equal and parallel. <!-- MQ-G10C17-A-PARA_MIDPO-01-S4 -->

$$\therefore APCQ\ \text{is a parallelogram}$$

**Final answer:** $APCQ\ \text{is a parallelogram}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

## G10C18 — Sets

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01-S1", "MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** n(A) = 25, n(B) = 14 and n(A ∩ B) = 6. Find n(A ∪ B).

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The overlap is counted twice when you add, so subtract it once.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Use the formula. <!-- MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01-S1 -->

$$n(A\cup B) = n(A) + n(B) - n(A\cap B)$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C18-F-SETS_UNION-01-S2 -->

$$= 25 + 14 - 6 = 33$$

**Final answer:** $33$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $33$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

$$= 30 + 22 - 10 = 42$$

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S1", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S2", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S3", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** In a class of 40 students, 22 play cricket, 18 play volleyball and 7 play both. How many play (i) only cricket (ii) neither game?

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Fill the Venn diagram from the middle outwards.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Start with the intersection in the Venn diagram. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S1 -->

$$n(C\cap V) = 7$$

Step 2 — Only cricket. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S2 -->

$$22 - 7 = 15$$

Step 3 — Only volleyball. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S3 -->

$$18 - 7 = 11$$

Step 4 — Neither = total − (union). <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-01-S4 -->

$$40 - (15 + 7 + 11) = 7$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 15\quad(ii)\ 7$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 15\quad(ii)\ 7$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S1", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S2", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S3", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** In a class of 50 students, 30 play cricket, 24 play volleyball and 12 play both. How many play (i) only cricket (ii) neither game?

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Fill the Venn diagram from the middle outwards.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Start with the intersection in the Venn diagram. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S1 -->

$$n(C\cap V) = 12$$

Step 2 — Only cricket. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S2 -->

$$30 - 12 = 18$$

Step 3 — Only volleyball. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S3 -->

$$24 - 12 = 12$$

Step 4 — Neither = total − (union). <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-02-S4 -->

$$50 - (18 + 12 + 12) = 8$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 18\quad(ii)\ 8$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 18\quad(ii)\ 8$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S1", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S2", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S3", "MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** In a class of 60 students, 35 play cricket, 28 play volleyball and 15 play both. How many play (i) only cricket (ii) neither game?

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Fill the Venn diagram from the middle outwards.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Start with the intersection in the Venn diagram. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S1 -->

$$n(C\cap V) = 15$$

Step 2 — Only cricket. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S2 -->

$$35 - 15 = 20$$

Step 3 — Only volleyball. <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S3 -->

$$28 - 15 = 13$$

Step 4 — Neither = total − (union). <!-- MQ-G10C18-G-SETS_NEITH-03-S4 -->

$$60 - (20 + 15 + 13) = 12$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 20\quad(ii)\ 12$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 20\quad(ii)\ 12$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S2", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** Of 60 people, 32 drink tea, 40 drink coffee and 6 drink neither. How many drink both? How many drink exactly one?

- **Hint 1:** Find n(T ∪ C) from 'neither'.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — People who drink at least one. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S1 -->

$$n(T\cup C) = 60 - 6 = 54$$

Step 2 — Rearrange the union formula. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S2 -->

$$n(T\cap C) = 32 + 40 - 54 = 18$$

Step 3 — Exactly one = union − both. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-01-S3 -->

$$54 - 18 = 36$$

**Final answer:** $\text{both} = 18,\ \text{exactly one} = 36$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{both} = 18,\ \text{exactly one} = 36$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S1", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S2", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** Of 45 people, 25 drink tea, 28 drink coffee and 4 drink neither. How many drink both? How many drink exactly one?

- **Hint 1:** Find n(T ∪ C) from 'neither'.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — People who drink at least one. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S1 -->

$$n(T\cup C) = 45 - 4 = 41$$

Step 2 — Rearrange the union formula. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S2 -->

$$n(T\cap C) = 25 + 28 - 41 = 12$$

Step 3 — Exactly one = union − both. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-02-S3 -->

$$41 - 12 = 29$$

**Final answer:** $\text{both} = 12,\ \text{exactly one} = 29$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{both} = 12,\ \text{exactly one} = 29$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C18", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S1", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S2", "MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** Of 50 people, 27 drink tea, 30 drink coffee and 3 drink neither. How many drink both? How many drink exactly one?

- **Hint 1:** Find n(T ∪ C) from 'neither'.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — People who drink at least one. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S1 -->

$$n(T\cup C) = 50 - 3 = 47$$

Step 2 — Rearrange the union formula. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S2 -->

$$n(T\cap C) = 27 + 30 - 47 = 10$$

Step 3 — Exactly one = union − both. <!-- MQ-G10C18-A-SETS_FIND_-03-S3 -->

$$47 - 10 = 37$$

**Final answer:** $\text{both} = 10,\ \text{exactly one} = 37$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{both} = 10,\ \text{exactly one} = 37$

## G10C19 — Logarithms I

#### MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C19", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-LOG-DEF", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01-S1", "MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Logarithms I · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-LOG-DEF`

**Question:** Write $\log_{5} 125 = 3$ in index form.

- **Hint 1:** The base of the log becomes the base of the power.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Rule: log_a N = x means a^x = N. <!-- MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01-S1 -->

$$\log_a N = x \iff a^x = N$$

Step 2 — Apply it. <!-- MQ-G10C19-F-LOG_INDEX_-01-S2 -->

$$5^{3} = 125$$

**Final answer:** $5^{3} = 125$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C19", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-LOG-LAWS", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S1", "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S2", "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Logarithms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-LOG-LAWS`

**Question:** Simplify without tables: $\lg 8 + \lg 125$

- **Hint 1:** lg means log base 10.
- **Hint 2:** log a + log b = log (ab).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add logs → multiply numbers. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S1 -->

$$\lg 8 + \lg 125 = \lg(8\times125)$$

Step 2 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S2 -->

$$= \lg 1000$$

Step 3 — 1000 = 10^3. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-01-S3 -->

$$= \lg 10^{3} = 3$$

**Final answer:** $3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C19", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-LOG-LAWS", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S1", "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S2", "MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Logarithms I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-LOG-LAWS`

**Question:** Simplify without tables: $\lg 50 + \lg 2$

- **Hint 1:** lg means log base 10.
- **Hint 2:** log a + log b = log (ab).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add logs → multiply numbers. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S1 -->

$$\lg 50 + \lg 2 = \lg(50\times2)$$

Step 2 — Multiply. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S2 -->

$$= \lg 100$$

Step 3 — 100 = 10^2. <!-- MQ-G10C19-G-LOG_LAWS-02-S3 -->

$$= \lg 10^{2} = 2$$

**Final answer:** $2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $2$

**Question:** Solve: $\log_{3}(x + 3) = 2$

$$x + 3 = 3^{2}$$

Step 2 — Evaluate the power. <!-- MQ-G10C19-A-LOG_SOLVE-01-S2 -->

$$x + 3 = 9$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C19-A-LOG_SOLVE-01-S3 -->

$$x = 6$$

**Topic:** Logarithms II · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-LOG-TAB`

**Question:** Write 520 in scientific notation and state the characteristic of lg 520.

- **Hint 1:** Count how many places the decimal point moves.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Scientific notation: A × 10ⁿ with 1 ≤ A < 10. <!-- MQ-G10C20-F-LOG_CHAR-01-S1 -->

$$520 = 5.2\times 10^{2}$$

Step 2 — The characteristic is the power of 10. <!-- MQ-G10C20-F-LOG_CHAR-01-S2 -->

$$\text{characteristic} = 2$$

Step 3 — The mantissa (from tables) is the same for 3.47, 34.7, 0.347 … <!-- MQ-G10C20-F-LOG_CHAR-01-S3 -->

$$\lg 520 = 2.7160$$

**Final answer:** $2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $2$

#### MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C20", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-LOG-TAB", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S1", "MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S2", "MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Logarithms II · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-LOG-TAB`

**Question:** Using logarithm tables, find the value of 25.6 × 1.75 (to 3 significant figures).

- **Hint 1:** Multiplication becomes addition of logs.
- **Hint 2:** Characteristic of the answer tells where the decimal point goes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let P = the product and take lg. <!-- MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S1 -->

$$\lg P = \lg 25.6 + \lg 1.75$$

Step 2 — Read the logs from the tables. <!-- MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S2 -->

$$\lg P = 1.4082 + 0.2430 = 1.6512$$

Step 3 — Find the antilog. <!-- MQ-G10C20-G-LOG_PRODUC-01-S3 -->

$$P = \text{antilog}\ 1.6512 \approx 44.8$$

**Final answer:** $44.8$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $44.8$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.448

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Multiplying the logs instead of adding them.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C20", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-LOG-TAB", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S3", "MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Logarithms II · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-LOG-TAB`

**Question:** Using logarithm tables, find the value of $\frac{0.042}{0.35}$ (to 3 significant figures).

- **Hint 1:** Bar numbers: only the characteristic is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Division → subtract logs. <!-- MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S1 -->

$$\lg P = \lg 0.042 - \lg 0.35$$

Step 2 — Read the logs (bar = negative characteristic). <!-- MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S2 -->

$$\lg P = \bar{2}.6232 - \bar{1}.5441$$

Step 3 — Subtract carefully (mantissa is always positive). <!-- MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S3 -->

$$\lg P = \bar{1}.0792$$

Step 4 — Antilog. <!-- MQ-G10C20-A-LOG_QUOTIE-01-S4 -->

$$P \approx 0.120$$

**Final answer:** $0.120$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $0.120$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.001

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Treating the whole bar number as negative.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C21-F-GRADIENT_I-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C21", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-LINE", "diagram": "GRA-LINE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C21-F-GRADIENT_I-01-S1", "MQ-G10C21-F-GRADIENT_I-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GRA-LINE`

**Question:** For the straight line $y = -3x  + 1$, write down the gradient and the y-intercept.

**Diagram:** `GRA-LINE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-LINE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The number multiplying x is the gradient.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare with y = mx + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-F-GRADIENT_I-01-S1 -->

$$y = -3x  + 1$$

Step 2 — m is the gradient, c is the intercept. <!-- MQ-G10C21-F-GRADIENT_I-01-S2 -->

$$m = -3,\quad c = 1$$

**Final answer:** $m=-3,\ c=1$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $m=-3,\ c=1$

#### MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C21", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-GRAD", "diagram": "GRA-LINE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S1", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S2", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-GRAD`

**Question:** Find the gradient of the line through (2, 1) and (6, 3), and its equation.

**Diagram:** `GRA-LINE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-LINE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Subtract in the same order for top and bottom.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Gradient = change in y ÷ change in x. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S1 -->

$$m = \frac{3 - (1)}{6 - (2)} = \frac{1}{2}$$

Step 2 — Substitute one point in y = mx + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S2 -->

$$1 = \frac{1}{2}\times(2) + c \Rightarrow c = 0$$

Step 3 — Write the equation. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-01-S3 -->

$$y = \frac{1}{2}x + 0$$

**Final answer:** $m=\frac{1}{2},\ y=\frac{1}{2}x + 0$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $m=\frac{1}{2},\ y=\frac{1}{2}x + 0$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C21", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-GRAD", "diagram": "GRA-LINE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S1", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S2", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-GRAD`

**Question:** Find the gradient of the line through (0, -2) and (2, 4), and its equation.

**Diagram:** `GRA-LINE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-LINE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Subtract in the same order for top and bottom.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Gradient = change in y ÷ change in x. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S1 -->

$$m = \frac{4 - (-2)}{2 - (0)} = 3$$

Step 2 — Substitute one point in y = mx + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S2 -->

$$-2 = 3\times(0) + c \Rightarrow c = -2$$

Step 3 — Write the equation. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-02-S3 -->

$$y = 3x - 2$$

**Final answer:** $m=3,\ y=3x - 2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $m=3,\ y=3x - 2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C21", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-GRAD", "diagram": "GRA-LINE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S1", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S2", "MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-GRAD`

**Question:** Find the gradient of the line through (1, 2) and (4, 8), and its equation.

**Diagram:** `GRA-LINE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-LINE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Subtract in the same order for top and bottom.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Gradient = change in y ÷ change in x. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S1 -->

$$m = \frac{8 - (2)}{4 - (1)} = 2$$

Step 2 — Substitute one point in y = mx + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S2 -->

$$2 = 2\times(1) + c \Rightarrow c = 0$$

Step 3 — Write the equation. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-03-S3 -->

$$y = 2x + 0$$

**Final answer:** $m=2,\ y=2x + 0$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $m=2,\ y=2x + 0$

**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-GRAD`

**Question:** Find the gradient of the line through (-1, 5) and (3, -3), and its equation.

**Diagram:** `GRA-LINE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-LINE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Subtract in the same order for top and bottom.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Gradient = change in y ÷ change in x. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-04-S1 -->

$$m = \frac{-3 - (5)}{3 - (-1)} = -2$$

Step 2 — Substitute one point in y = mx + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-04-S2 -->

$$5 = -2\times(-1) + c \Rightarrow c = 3$$

Step 3 — Write the equation. <!-- MQ-G10C21-G-GRADIENT_T-04-S3 -->

$$y = -2x + 3$$

**Final answer:** $m=-2,\ y=-2x + 3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $m=-2,\ y=-2x + 3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C21", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-02", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S1", "MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S2", "MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-QUAD`

**Question:** For $y = 2x^{2} - 8$: (i) complete a table for x = −3 to 3, (ii) state the turning point and whether it is a maximum or minimum, (iii) find where the graph cuts the x-axis.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-02` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Square x first, then multiply by a: (−2)² = 4.
- **Hint 2:** If a > 0 the curve opens upward (minimum).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute each x value. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3\\ \hline y & 10 & 0 & -6 & -8 & -6 & 0 & 10\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Turning point is at x = 0 for y = ax² + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S2 -->

$$(0,\ -8),\ \text{minimum}\ (a > 0)$$

Step 3 — Graph cuts the x-axis where y = 0. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-01-S3 -->

$$2x^{2} - 8 = 0 \Rightarrow x^2 = 4 \Rightarrow x = \pm 2$$

**Final answer:** $(0,-8)\ \text{minimum};\ x=\pm 2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(0,-8)\ \text{minimum};\ x=\pm 2$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Writing (−3)² as −9.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-02

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-02` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Square x first, then multiply by a: (−2)² = 4.
- **Hint 2:** If a > 0 the curve opens upward (minimum).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute each x value. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-02-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3\\ \hline y & -5 & 0 & 3 & 4 & 3 & 0 & -5\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Turning point is at x = 0 for y = ax² + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-02-S2 -->

$$(0,\ 4),\ \text{maximum}\ (a < 0)$$

Step 3 — Graph cuts the x-axis where y = 0. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-02-S3 -->

$$-x^{2} + 4 = 0 \Rightarrow x^2 = 4 \Rightarrow x = \pm 2$$

**Final answer:** $(0,4)\ \text{maximum};\ x=\pm 2$

- **Hint 2:** If a > 0 the curve opens upward (minimum).

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute each x value. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-03-S1 -->

Step 2 — Turning point is at x = 0 for y = ax² + c. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-03-S2 -->

$$(0,\ -9),\ \text{minimum}\ (a > 0)$$

Step 3 — Graph cuts the x-axis where y = 0. <!-- MQ-G10C21-A-QUAD_AX2C-03-S3 -->

## G10C22 — Rate

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C22", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-SPEED", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01-S1", "MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Rate · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-SPEED`

**Question:** A bus travels 75 km at an average speed of 50 km/h. How long does it take?

- **Hint 1:** Speed = distance/time, so time = distance/speed.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Time = distance ÷ speed. <!-- MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01-S1 -->

$$t = \frac{75}{50} = \frac{3}{2}\ \text{h}$$

Step 2 — Convert to hours and minutes. <!-- MQ-G10C22-F-SPEED_TIME-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{3}{2}\ \text{h} = 1\ \text{h}\ 30\ \text{min}$$

**Final answer:** $1\ \text{h}\ 30\ \text{min}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $1\ \text{h}\ 30\ \text{min}$ · **Unit:** h

**Topic:** Rate · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-NUM-RATE`

**Question:** A tank holds 3600 litres. Water flows in at 60 litres per minute. How long will it take to fill the empty tank?

- **Hint 1:** Rate tells how many litres each minute.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Time = volume ÷ rate. <!-- MQ-G10C22-G-TANK_FILL-01-S1 -->

$$t = 3600\div 60 = 60\ \text{min}$$

Step 2 — Convert if needed. <!-- MQ-G10C22-G-TANK_FILL-01-S2 -->

$$60\ \text{min} = 1\ \text{h}\ 0\ \text{min}$$

**Final answer:** $60\ \text{min}$

#### MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C22", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-NUM-RATE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01-S1", "MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Rate · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-NUM-RATE`

**Question:** An empty tank of 1800 L is filled by a pipe at 45 L/min while a leak removes 15 L/min. How long does it take to fill the tank?

- **Hint 1:** The leak works against the pipe.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Net rate = in − out. <!-- MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01-S1 -->

$$45 - 15 = 30\ \text{L/min}$$

Step 2 — Time = volume ÷ net rate. <!-- MQ-G10C22-A-TWO_PIPES-01-S2 -->

$$1800\div 30 = 60\ \text{min}$$

**Final answer:** $60\ \text{min}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $60\ \text{min}$ · **Unit:** min

## G10C23 — Formulae

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C23", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-SUBJ", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01-S1", "MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Formulae · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SUBJ`

**Question:** Given v = u + at, find v when u = 5, a = 2 and t = 3.

- **Hint 1:** BODMAS: multiply before adding.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute the values. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01-S1 -->

$$v = 5 + (2)\times 3$$

Step 2 — Multiply first, then add. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-01-S2 -->

$$v = 5 + (6) = 11$$

**Final answer:** $v = 11$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $v = 11$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

- **Hint 1:** BODMAS: multiply before adding.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute the values. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-02-S1 -->

$$v = 8 + (-2)\times 3$$

Step 2 — Multiply first, then add. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-02-S2 -->

$$v = 8 + (-6) = 2$$

**Final answer:** $v = 2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $v = 2$

- **Hint 1:** BODMAS: multiply before adding.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Substitute the values. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-03-S1 -->

$$v = 10 + (3)\times 4$$

Step 2 — Multiply first, then add. <!-- MQ-G10C23-F-SUBST_FORM-03-S2 -->

$$v = 10 + (12) = 22$$

**Final answer:** $v = 22$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $v = 22$

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C23", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-SUBJ", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S1", "MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S2", "MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Formulae · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SUBJ`

**Question:** Make r the subject of $A = \pi r^2$, and find r when A = 154 and π = 22/7.

- **Hint 1:** Undo the operations in reverse order.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Divide both sides by π. <!-- MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S1 -->

$$r^2 = \frac{A}{\pi}$$

Step 2 — Take the square root. <!-- MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S2 -->

$$r = \sqrt{\frac{A}{\pi}}$$

Step 3 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C23-G-CHANGE_SUB-01-S3 -->

$$r = \sqrt{154\times\frac{7}{22}} = \sqrt{49} = 7$$

**Final answer:** $r=\sqrt{\frac{A}{\pi}},\ r=7$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $r=\sqrt{\frac{A}{\pi}},\ r=7$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C23", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-SUBJ", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S1", "MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S2", "MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S3", "MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S4", "MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Formulae · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-SUBJ`

**Question:** Make x the subject of $y = \frac{2x + 3}{x - 1}$.

- **Hint 1:** x appears twice — collect, then factorise x out.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply both sides by (x − 1). <!-- MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S1 -->

$$y(x-1) = 2x+3$$

Step 2 — Expand. <!-- MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S2 -->

$$xy - y = 2x + 3$$

Step 3 — Collect x terms on one side. <!-- MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S3 -->

$$xy - 2x = y + 3$$

Step 4 — Factorise x. <!-- MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S4 -->

$$x(y-2) = y+3$$

Step 5 — Divide. <!-- MQ-G10C23-A-CHANGE_SUB-01-S5 -->

$$x = \frac{y+3}{y-2}$$

**Final answer:** $x=\frac{y+3}{y-2}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G10C24 — Arithmetic Progressions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C24", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-AP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S1", "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S2", "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Arithmetic Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-AP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 15th term of the arithmetic progression 5, 8, 11, …

- **Hint 1:** d = second term − first term.
- **Hint 2:** Use (n − 1), not n.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a and d. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S1 -->

$$a = 5,\quad d = 8 - 5 = 3$$

Step 2 — Use Tₙ = a + (n − 1)d. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S2 -->

$$T_{15} = 5 + (15-1)\times(3)$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-01-S3 -->

$$T_{15} = 5 + 42 = 47$$

**Final answer:** $47$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $47$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C24", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-AP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S1", "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S2", "MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Arithmetic Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-AP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 12th term of the arithmetic progression 7, 12, 17, …

- **Hint 1:** d = second term − first term.
- **Hint 2:** Use (n − 1), not n.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a and d. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S1 -->

$$a = 7,\quad d = 12 - 7 = 5$$

Step 2 — Use Tₙ = a + (n − 1)d. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S2 -->

$$T_{12} = 7 + (12-1)\times(5)$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G10C24-F-AP_NTH-02-S3 -->

$$T_{12} = 7 + 55 = 62$$

**Final answer:** $62$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $62$
