## G11C08 — Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "diagram": "GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-AREA-PAR`

**Question:** ΔABC and ΔDBC are on the same base BC and between the same parallel lines (AD ∥ BC). Area of ΔABC = 24 cm². Find the area of ΔDBC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Area of a triangle = ½ × base × height.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Same base, same height (distance between parallels). <!-- MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01-S1 -->

$$h_{ABC} = h_{DBC}$$

Step 2 — So the areas are equal. <!-- MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01-S2 -->

$$\text{Area }\triangle DBC = 24\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $24\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $24\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "diagram": "GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01-S1", "MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-AREA-PAR`

**Question:** Parallelogram PQRS and ΔPQT are on the same base PQ and between the same parallels. The area of PQRS is 90 cm². Find the area of ΔPQT.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Parallelogram = base × height; triangle = ½ × base × height.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — A triangle is half the parallelogram on the same base and between the same parallels. <!-- MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01-S1 -->

$$\triangle = \tfrac12\ \text{parallelogram}$$

Step 2 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01-S2 -->

$$\tfrac12\times 90 = 45\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $45\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $45\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "diagram": "GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S1", "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S2", "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-AREA-PAR`

**Question:** In parallelogram ABCD, E is any point on DC. Prove that area ΔAEB = area ΔADE + area ΔBCE.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Compare each part with the whole parallelogram.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ΔAEB and parallelogram ABCD: same base AB, between AB ∥ DC. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S1 -->

$$\triangle AEB = \tfrac12\,ABCD$$

Step 2 — The rest of the parallelogram is the other half. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S2 -->

$$\triangle ADE + \triangle BCE = ABCD - \triangle AEB = \tfrac12\,ABCD$$

Step 3 — Both equal half the parallelogram. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S3 -->

$$\therefore \triangle AEB = \triangle ADE + \triangle BCE$$

**Final answer:** $\triangle AEB = \triangle ADE + \triangle BCE$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-RATES`

**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 40 000. The council charges rates of 10%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **Hint 1:** Rates are a percentage of the ANNUAL value, paid in four quarters.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Annual rates. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S1 -->

$$40\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 4000$$

Step 2 — One quarter = ÷ 4. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S2 -->

$$4000\div 4 = 1000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }1000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }1000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S2", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S3", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S4", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 100 000 is deposited at 8% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S1 -->

$$100\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S2 -->

$$100\,000 + 8000 = 108\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S3 -->

$$108\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8640$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S4 -->

$$108\,000 + 8640 = 116\,640$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S5 -->

$$116\,640 - 100\,000 = 16\,640$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Calculating year-2 interest on the original amount (that is simple interest).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S1", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S2", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S3", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S4", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 40 000 is deposited at 5% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S1 -->

$$40\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S2 -->

$$40\,000 + 2000 = 42\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S3 -->

$$42\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2100$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S4 -->

$$42\,000 + 2100 = 44\,100$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S5 -->

$$44\,100 - 40\,000 = 4100$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Calculating year-2 interest on the original amount (that is simple interest).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-TAX", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S2", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S3", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S4", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-TAX`

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 2 400 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S1 -->

$$2\,400\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 1\,200\,000$$

Step 2 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 6%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S2 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 30\,000$$

Step 3 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 12%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S3 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{12}{100} = 60\,000$$

Step 4 — Tax on Rs. 200 000 at 18%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S4 -->

$$200\,000\times\frac{18}{100} = 36\,000$$

Step 5 — Total tax. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S5 -->

$$30\,000 + 60\,000 + 36\,000 = 126\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }126\,000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }126\,000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Applying the highest rate to the whole income.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-TAX", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S1", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S2", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-TAX`

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 1 500 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S1 -->

$$1\,500\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 300\,000$$

Step 2 — Tax on Rs. 300 000 at 6%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S2 -->

$$300\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 18\,000$$

Step 3 — Total tax. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S3 -->

$$18\,000 = 18\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }18\,000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }18\,000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Applying the highest rate to the whole income.

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-TAX", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S1", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S2", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S3", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S4"]} -->

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 1 800 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S1 -->

$$1\,800\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 600\,000$$

Step 2 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 6%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S2 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 30\,000$$

Step 3 — Tax on Rs. 100 000 at 12%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S3 -->

$$100\,000\times\frac{12}{100} = 12\,000$$

Step 4 — Total tax. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S4 -->

$$30\,000 + 12\,000 = 42\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }42\,000$

## G11C10 — Share Market

#### MQ-G11C10-F-BUY_SHARES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C10-F-BUY_SHARES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C10", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-SHARE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C10-F-BUY_SHARES-01-S1"]} -->
**Topic:** Share Market · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SHARE`

**Question:** How many shares priced at Rs. 12 each can be bought with Rs. 36 000?

- **Hint 1:** Divide the money by the price of one share.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Number of shares = money ÷ price per share. <!-- MQ-G11C10-F-BUY_SHARES-01-S1 -->

$$36\,000\div12 = 3000$$

**Final answer:** $3000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3000$

<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }6000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

#### MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C10", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-SHARE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Share Market · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SHARE`

**Question:** Kamal has Rs. 90 000. Option A: buy shares at Rs. 45 each paying an annual dividend of Rs. 4 per share. Option B: deposit in a bank at 9% simple interest per year. Which gives the higher income in one year, and by how much?

- **Hint 1:** Find both incomes, then subtract.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Number of shares. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S1 -->

$$90\,000\div 45 = 2000$$

Step 2 — Dividend income. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S2 -->

$$2000\times4 = 8000$$

Step 3 — Bank interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S3 -->

$$90\,000\times\frac{9}{100} = 8100$$

Step 4 — Compare. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S4 -->

$$|8000 - 8100| = 100$$

**Final answer:** $\text{bank},\ \text{Rs. }100$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{bank},\ \text{Rs. }100$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

- **Hint 1:** Find both incomes, then subtract.

$$60\,000\div 30 = 2000$$

Step 2 — Dividend income. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-02-S2 -->

$$2000\times3 = 6000$$

Step 3 — Bank interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-02-S3 -->

- **Hint 1:** Find both incomes, then subtract.

## G11C11 — Mid-point Theorem

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C11", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S1", "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Mid-point Theorem · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-MID`

**Question:** In ΔABC, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and AC. BC = 10 cm. Find PQ.

**Diagram:** `GEO-MIDPT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-MIDPT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Join of midpoints = half the third side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Mid-point theorem: PQ ∥ BC and PQ = ½ BC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S1 -->

$$PQ = \tfrac12 BC$$

Step 2 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S2 -->

$$PQ = \tfrac12\times 10 = 5\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $5\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $5\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C11", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01-S1", "MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Mid-point Theorem · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-MID`

**Question:** The sides of ΔABC are AB = 7 cm, BC = 9 cm and CA = 12 cm. P, Q, R are the midpoints of the sides. Find the perimeter of ΔPQR.

**Diagram:** `GEO-MIDPT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-MIDPT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Use the mid-point theorem three times.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Each side of ΔPQR is half of a side of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01-S1 -->

$$\tfrac12(7),\ \tfrac12(9),\ \tfrac12(12)$$

Step 2 — Perimeter = half of the big perimeter. <!-- MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01-S2 -->

$$\tfrac12(7+9+12) = 14\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $14\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $14\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C11", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S1", "MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Mid-point Theorem · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-MID`

**Question:** In ΔABC, D is the midpoint of AB. A line through D parallel to BC meets AC at E. If AC = 14 cm and BC = 10 cm, find AE and DE. Give reasons.

**Diagram:** `GEO-MIDPT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-MIDPT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** A line from a midpoint parallel to a side bisects the third side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Converse of the mid-point theorem: E is the midpoint of AC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S1 -->

$$AE = \tfrac12 AC = 7\ \text{cm}$$

Step 2 — Then DE = ½ BC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S2 -->

$$DE = \tfrac12\times 10 = 5\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $AE = 7\text{ cm},\ DE = 5\text{ cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $AE = 7\text{ cm},\ DE = 5\text{ cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

## G11C12 — Graphs

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S1", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S2", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** For $y = (x  - 1)^2  + 4$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The sign inside the bracket flips for the x-coordinate.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S1 -->

$$(-(-1),\ 4) = (1,\ 4)$$

Step 2 — Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S2 -->

$$x = 1$$

Step 3 — (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-01-S3 -->

$$y_{\min} = 4$$

**Final answer:** $(1,4),\ x=1,\ y_{\min}=4$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(1,4),\ x=1,\ y_{\min}=4$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S1", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S2", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** For $y = (x  - 2)^2  - 5$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The sign inside the bracket flips for the x-coordinate.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S1 -->

$$(-(-2),\ -5) = (2,\ -5)$$

Step 2 — Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S2 -->

$$x = 2$$

Step 3 — (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02-S3 -->

$$y_{\min} = -5$$

**Final answer:** $(2,-5),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-5$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(2,-5),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-5$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S1", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S2", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** For $y = (x  + 2)^2  - 3$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The sign inside the bracket flips for the x-coordinate.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S1 -->

$$(-(2),\ -3) = (-2,\ -3)$$

Step 2 — Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S2 -->

$$x = -2$$

Step 3 — (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03-S3 -->

$$y_{\min} = -3$$

**Final answer:** $(-2,-3),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(-2,-3),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-3$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S1", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S2", "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** For $y = (x  + 3)^2  - 1$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The sign inside the bracket flips for the x-coordinate.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S1 -->

$$(-(3),\ -1) = (-3,\ -1)$$

Step 2 — Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S2 -->

$$x = -3$$

Step 3 — (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. <!-- MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04-S3 -->

$$y_{\min} = -1$$

**Final answer:** $(-3,-1),\ x=-3,\ y_{\min}=-1$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(-3,-1),\ x=-3,\ y_{\min}=-1$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S1", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S2", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S3", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-QUAD`

**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 2x - 3$, prepare a table of values for x from -2 to 4, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 2x - 3 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for y = 0 in your table.
- **Hint 2:** The curve is symmetric about the turning point.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Table of values. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S2 -->

$$x = -1,\ x = 3$$

Step 3 — Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-1+3}{2} = 1$$

Step 4 — Substitute to get y. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S4 -->

$$y = -4 \Rightarrow (1,\ -4)$$

**Final answer:** $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S1", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S2", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S3", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-QUAD`

**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 4$, prepare a table of values for x from -3 to 3, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 4 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for y = 0 in your table.
- **Hint 2:** The curve is symmetric about the turning point.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Table of values. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S2 -->

$$x = -2,\ x = 2$$

Step 3 — Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-2+2}{2} = 0$$

Step 4 — Substitute to get y. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02-S4 -->

$$y = -4 \Rightarrow (0,\ -4)$$

**Final answer:** $x=-2,\ 2;\ (0,-4)$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=-2,\ 2;\ (0,-4)$

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for y = 0 in your table.
- **Hint 2:** The curve is symmetric about the turning point.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Table of values. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -4 & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03-S2 -->

$$x = -3,\ x = 1$$

Step 3 — Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-3+1}{2} = -1$$

Step 4 — Substitute to get y. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03-S4 -->

#### MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S1", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S2", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S3", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-QUAD`

**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 5x + 4$, prepare a table of values for x from 0 to 5, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 5x + 4 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for y = 0 in your table.
- **Hint 2:** The curve is symmetric about the turning point.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Table of values. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|cccccc} x & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & 5\\ \hline y & 4 & 0 & -2 & -2 & 0 & 4\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S2 -->

$$x = 1,\ x = 4$$

Step 3 — Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{1+4}{2} = \frac{5}{2}$$

Step 4 — Substitute to get y. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-04-S4 -->

$$y = -\frac{9}{4} \Rightarrow (\frac{5}{2},\ -\frac{9}{4})$$

**Final answer:** $x=1,\ 4;\ (\frac{5}{2},-\frac{9}{4})$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=1,\ 4;\ (\frac{5}{2},-\frac{9}{4})$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} + 4x + 1$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{4}{2} = 2$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S2 -->

$$y = (x + 2)^2 - (2)^2  + 1$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S3 -->

$$y = (x + 2)^2 - 3$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S4 -->

$$(-2,\ -3),\quad x < -2$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x + 2)^2 - 3;\ (-2,-3);\ x<-2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x + 2)^2 - 3;\ (-2,-3);\ x<-2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} - 4x - 1$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S1 -->

$$\frac{-4}{2} = -2$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S2 -->

$$y = (x - 2)^2 - (-2)^2  - 1$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S3 -->

$$y = (x - 2)^2 - 5$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S4 -->

$$(2,\ -5),\quad x < 2$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x - 2)^2 - 5;\ (2,-5);\ x<2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x - 2)^2 - 5;\ (2,-5);\ x<2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} + 2x - 2$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S1 -->

$$\frac{2}{2} = 1$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S2 -->

$$y = (x + 1)^2 - (1)^2  - 2$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S3 -->

$$y = (x + 1)^2 - 3$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S4 -->

$$(-1,\ -3),\quad x < -1$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x + 1)^2 - 3;\ (-1,-3);\ x<-1$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x + 1)^2 - 3;\ (-1,-3);\ x<-1$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G11C13 — Equations

#### MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} - 7x + 10 = 0$

- **Hint 1:** If A × B = 0, then A = 0 or B = 0.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1 -->

$$(x  - 2)(x  - 5) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2 -->

$$x  - 2 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  - 5 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3 -->

$$x = 2\ \text{or}\ x = 5$$

**Final answer:** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S1 -->

$$(x  - 1)(x  + 6) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S2 -->

$$x  - 1 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  + 6 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S3 -->

$$x = 1\ \text{or}\ x = -6$$

**Final answer:** $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$

**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} - x - 12 = 0$

- **Hint 1:** If A × B = 0, then A = 0 or B = 0.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04-S1 -->

$$(x  + 3)(x  - 4) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04-S2 -->

$$x  + 3 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  - 4 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04-S3 -->

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-QFORM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S3", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QFORM`

**Question:** Solve $x^{2} + 2x - 4 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **Hint 1:** x = [−b ± √(b² − 4ac)] / 2a
- **Hint 2:** Be careful with −b when b is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a, b, c. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S1 -->

$$a = 1,\ b = 2,\ c = -4$$

Step 2 — Discriminant b² − 4ac. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S2 -->

$$(2)^2 - 4(1)(-4) = 20$$

Step 3 — Formula. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-(2) \pm \sqrt{20}}{2\times1},\quad \sqrt{20} \approx 4.472$$

Step 4 — Two answers. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S4 -->

$$x \approx 1.24\ \text{or}\ x \approx -3.24$$

**Final answer:** $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-QFORM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S1", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S2", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S3", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QFORM`

**Question:** Solve $x^{2} - 4x + 1 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **Hint 1:** x = [−b ± √(b² − 4ac)] / 2a
- **Hint 2:** Be careful with −b when b is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a, b, c. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S1 -->

$$a = 1,\ b = -4,\ c = 1$$

Step 2 — Discriminant b² − 4ac. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S2 -->

$$(-4)^2 - 4(1)(1) = 12$$

Step 3 — Formula. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-(-4) \pm \sqrt{12}}{2\times1},\quad \sqrt{12} \approx 3.464$$

Step 4 — Two answers. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02-S4 -->

$$x \approx 3.73\ \text{or}\ x \approx 0.27$$

**Final answer:** $x \approx 3.73,\ 0.27$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x \approx 3.73,\ 0.27$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Question:** Solve $3x^{2} - 5x + 1 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **Hint 1:** x = [−b ± √(b² − 4ac)] / 2a
- **Hint 2:** Be careful with −b when b is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a, b, c. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-03-S1 -->

$$a = 3,\ b = -5,\ c = 1$$

Step 2 — Discriminant b² − 4ac. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-03-S2 -->

$$(-5)^2 - 4(3)(1) = 13$$

Step 3 — Formula. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-03-S3 -->

$$x \approx 1.43\ \text{or}\ x \approx 0.23$$

**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QFORM`

**Question:** Solve $2x^{2} + 3x - 1 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **Hint 1:** x = [−b ± √(b² − 4ac)] / 2a
- **Hint 2:** Be careful with −b when b is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a, b, c. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-04-S1 -->

$$a = 2,\ b = 3,\ c = -1$$

Step 2 — Discriminant b² − 4ac. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-04-S2 -->

$$(3)^2 - 4(2)(-1) = 17$$

Step 3 — Formula. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-04-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-(3) \pm \sqrt{17}}{2\times2},\quad \sqrt{17} \approx 4.123$$

Step 4 — Two answers. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-04-S4 -->

$$x \approx 0.28\ \text{or}\ x \approx -1.78$$

**Final answer:** $x \approx 0.28,\ -1.78$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x \approx 0.28,\ -1.78$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

#### MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 3 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 54 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S1 -->

$$x(x+3) = 54$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 3x - 54 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S3 -->

$$(x-6)(x+9) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 6$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S4 -->

$$(6+2)\times(9+2) = 88$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S5 -->

$$88 - 54 = 34\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=6\text{ m},\ \text{path}=34\text{ m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=6\text{ m},\ \text{path}=34\text{ m}^2$ · **Unit:** m

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S5"]} -->

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 3 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 88 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S1 -->

$$x(x+3) = 88$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 3x - 88 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S3 -->

$$(x-8)(x+11) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 8$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S4 -->

$$(8+2)\times(11+2) = 130$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S5 -->

$$130 - 88 = 42\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=8\text{ m},\ \text{path}=42\text{ m}^2$

#### MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 2 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 24 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S1 -->

$$x(x+2) = 24$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 2x - 24 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S3 -->

$$(x-4)(x+6) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 4$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S4 -->

$$(4+2)\times(6+2) = 48$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S5 -->

$$48 - 24 = 24\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=4\text{ m},\ \text{path}=24\text{ m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=4\text{ m},\ \text{path}=24\text{ m}^2$ · **Unit:** m

## G11C14 — Equiangular Triangles

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S1", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S2", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 3 cm, BC = 5 cm and PQ = 9 cm. Find QR.

**Diagram:** `GEO-SIMILAR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SIMILAR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the scale factor PQ ÷ AB first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{9}{3} = \frac{QR}{5}$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01-S3 -->

$$QR = 5\times3 = 15\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $15\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $15\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S1", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S2", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 6 cm, BC = 9 cm and PQ = 3 cm. Find QR.

**Diagram:** `GEO-SIMILAR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SIMILAR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the scale factor PQ ÷ AB first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S1 -->

$$\frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{3}{6} = \frac{QR}{9}$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02-S3 -->

$$QR = 9\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{9}{2}\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{9}{2}\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{9}{2}\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

- **Hint 1:** Find the scale factor PQ ÷ AB first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-03-S1 -->

$$\frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC}$$

$$\frac{8}{4} = \frac{QR}{6}$$

#### MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S1", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S2", "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 5 cm, BC = 7 cm and PQ = \frac{15}{2} cm. Find QR.

**Diagram:** `GEO-SIMILAR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SIMILAR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the scale factor PQ ÷ AB first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S1 -->

$$\frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC}$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S2 -->

$$\frac{\frac{15}{2}}{5} = \frac{QR}{7}$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04-S3 -->

$$QR = 7\times\frac{3}{2} = \frac{21}{2}\ \text{cm}$$

#### MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S1", "MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S2", "MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** In ΔABC, DE ∥ BC with D on AB and E on AC. AD = 4 cm, DB = 2 cm and DE = 6 cm. Find BC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-SIMILAR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-SIMILAR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Use AB (the whole side), not DB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ΔADE and ΔABC are equiangular (corresponding angles, DE ∥ BC). <!-- MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S1 -->

$$\triangle ADE \sim \triangle ABC$$

Step 2 — AB = AD + DB. <!-- MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S2 -->

$$AB = 4+2 = 6$$

Step 3 — Ratio of sides. <!-- MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{BC}{DE} = \frac{AB}{AD} \Rightarrow BC = 6\times\frac{6}{4} = 9\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $9\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $9\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

- **Hint 1:** Use AB (the whole side), not DB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ΔADE and ΔABC are equiangular (corresponding angles, DE ∥ BC). <!-- MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-02-S1 -->

$$\triangle ADE \sim \triangle ABC$$

Step 2 — AB = AD + DB. <!-- MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-02-S2 -->

$$AB = 3+6 = 9$$

- **Hint 1:** Use AB (the whole side), not DB.

**Step-by-step solution**

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-CHORDS-INT-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S1", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S2", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S3", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S4", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** AB and CD are two chords of a circle that intersect at X inside the circle. Prove that ΔAXC and ΔDXB are equiangular and hence that AX × XB = CX × XD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CHORDS-INT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CHORDS-INT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for vertically opposite angles and same-segment angles.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Vertically opposite angles. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S1 -->

$$\angle AXC = \angle DXB$$

Step 2 — Angles in the same segment (on arc BC... arc AD). <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ACX = \angle ACD = \angle ABD = \angle DBX$$

Step 3 — Third angles are equal (angle sum). <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S3 -->

$$\angle CAX = \angle BDX$$

Step 4 — So the triangles are equiangular; sides are proportional. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{AX}{DX} = \frac{CX}{BX}$$

Step 5 — Cross-multiply. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S5 -->

$$AX\times XB = CX\times XD$$

**Final answer:** $AX\times XB = CX\times XD$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-STA-MEDIAN`

**Question:** Find the median of: 18, 21, 22, 23, 25, 53, 56

- **Hint 1:** Always order the data first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — The data is already in order. Count the values. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S1 -->

$$n = 7$$

Step 2 — Median position = (n + 1)/2. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{7+1}{2} = 4$$

Step 3 — Read that value. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S3 -->

$$\text{median} = 23$$

**Final answer:** $23$

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S1", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S2", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-STA-QUART`

**Question:** For the 11 values 6, 28, 29, 30, 32, 38, 43, 48, 51, 59, 74, find the lower quartile, the upper quartile and the interquartile range.

- **Hint 1:** Positions: (n+1)/4 and 3(n+1)/4.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Q₁ position = (n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{11+1}{4} = 3 \Rightarrow Q_1 = 29$$

Step 2 — Q₃ position = 3(n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{3(11+1)}{4} = 9 \Rightarrow Q_3 = 51$$

Step 3 — Interquartile range = Q₃ − Q₁. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S3 -->

$$51 - 29 = 22$$

**Final answer:** $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": "STA-OGIVE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S1", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S2", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S3", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-STA-QUART`

**Question:** Classes 0–10 … 40–50 have frequencies 5, 10, 15, 6, 4. (i) Make a cumulative frequency table. (ii) Using the cumulative frequency curve (or interpolation), estimate the median.

**Diagram:** `STA-OGIVE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Statistical_Charts/STA-OGIVE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Read across from N/2 on the vertical axis to the curve, then down.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Cumulative frequencies (plot at upper boundaries 10, 20, …). <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S1 -->

$$5, 15, 30, 36, 40$$

Step 2 — Median is at N/2. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{40}{2} = 20$$

Step 3 — It lies in the class 20–30. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S3 -->

$$\text{c.f. before} = 15,\ f = 15$$

Step 4 — Interpolate. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-01-S4 -->

$$20 + \frac{20-15}{15}\times 10 \approx 23.3$$

**Final answer:** $\approx 23.3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\approx 23.3$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.6

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": "STA-OGIVE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S1", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S2", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S3", "MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S4"]} -->

- **Hint 1:** Read across from N/2 on the vertical axis to the curve, then down.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Cumulative frequencies (plot at upper boundaries 10, 20, …). <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S1 -->

$$4, 12, 28, 36, 40$$

Step 2 — Median is at N/2. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{40}{2} = 20$$

Step 3 — It lies in the class 20–30. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S3 -->

$$\text{c.f. before} = 12,\ f = 16$$

Step 4 — Interpolate. <!-- MQ-G11C15-A-OGIVE_READ-02-S4 -->

## G11C16 — Geometric Progressions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 5th term of the geometric progression 2, 6, 18, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S1 -->

$$r = 3$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S2 -->

$$T_{5} = 2\times(3)^{4}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S3 -->

$$= 2\times 81 = 162$$

**Final answer:** $162$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $162$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 7th term of the geometric progression 5, 10, 20, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S1 -->

$$r = 2$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S2 -->

$$T_{7} = 5\times(2)^{6}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S3 -->

$$= 5\times 64 = 320$$

**Final answer:** $320$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $320$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 4th term of the geometric progression 1, 4, 16, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S1 -->

$$r = 4$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S2 -->

$$T_{4} = 1\times(4)^{3}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S3 -->

$$= 1\times 64 = 64$$

**Final answer:** $64$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $64$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 6th term of the geometric progression 3, 6, 12, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S1 -->

$$r = 2$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S2 -->

$$T_{6} = 3\times(2)^{5}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-04-S3 -->

$$= 3\times 32 = 96$$

**Final answer:** $96$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $96$

**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GP-SN`

**Question:** Find the sum of the first 8 terms of the GP 1, 2, 4, …

- **Hint 1:** Work out rⁿ first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — a and r. <!-- MQ-G11C16-G-GP_SUM-01-S1 -->

$$a = 1,\ r = 2$$

Step 2 — Sₙ = a(rⁿ − 1)/(r − 1) for r > 1. <!-- MQ-G11C16-G-GP_SUM-01-S2 -->

$$S_{8} = \frac{1(2^{8}-1)}{2-1}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-G-GP_SUM-01-S3 -->

$$= \frac{1\times 255}{1} = 255$$

**Final answer:** $255$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $255$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S1", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S2", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S3", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** The 3rd term of a GP is 18 and the 6th term is 486. Find the first term and the common ratio.

- **Hint 1:** Divide one equation by the other.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write both terms. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S1 -->

$$ar^2 = 18,\quad ar^5 = 486$$

Step 2 — Divide to remove a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S2 -->

$$r^3 = \frac{486}{18} = 27$$

Step 3 — Cube root. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S3 -->

$$r = 3$$

Step 4 — Find a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-01-S4 -->

$$a\times 3^2 = 18 \Rightarrow a = 2$$

**Final answer:** $a=2,\ r=3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $a=2,\ r=3$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S1", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S2", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S3", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** The 3rd term of a GP is 36 and the 6th term is 972. Find the first term and the common ratio.

- **Hint 1:** Divide one equation by the other.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write both terms. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S1 -->

$$ar^2 = 36,\quad ar^5 = 972$$

Step 2 — Divide to remove a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S2 -->

$$r^3 = \frac{972}{36} = 27$$

Step 3 — Cube root. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S3 -->

$$r = 3$$

Step 4 — Find a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-02-S4 -->

$$a\times 3^2 = 36 \Rightarrow a = 4$$

**Final answer:** $a=4,\ r=3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $a=4,\ r=3$

#### MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S1", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S2", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S3", "MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** The 3rd term of a GP is 12 and the 6th term is 96. Find the first term and the common ratio.

- **Hint 1:** Divide one equation by the other.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write both terms. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S1 -->

$$ar^2 = 12,\quad ar^5 = 96$$

Step 2 — Divide to remove a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S2 -->

$$r^3 = \frac{96}{12} = 8$$

Step 3 — Cube root. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S3 -->

$$r = 2$$

Step 4 — Find a. <!-- MQ-G11C16-A-GP_FIND_R-03-S4 -->

$$a\times 2^2 = 12 \Rightarrow a = 3$$

**Final answer:** $a=3,\ r=2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $a=3,\ r=2$

## G11C17 — Pythagoras' Theorem

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S1", "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** The two shorter sides of a right-angled triangle are 6 cm and 8 cm. Find the hypotenuse.

**Diagram:** `GEO-RIGHT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-RIGHT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The hypotenuse is opposite the right angle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — c² = a² + b². <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S1 -->

$$c^2 = 6^2 + 8^2 = 36 + 64 = 100$$

Step 2 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S2 -->

$$c = \sqrt{100} = 10\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $10\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $10\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02

**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** The two shorter sides of a right-angled triangle are 8 cm and 15 cm. Find the hypotenuse.

- **Hint 1:** The hypotenuse is opposite the right angle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — c² = a² + b². <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02-S1 -->

$$c^2 = 8^2 + 15^2 = 64 + 225 = 289$$

Step 2 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02-S2 -->

$$c = \sqrt{289} = 17\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $17\ \text{cm}$

#### MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S1", "MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S2", "MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** A 25 m ladder leans against a vertical wall. Its foot is 7 m from the wall. How high up the wall does it reach?

**Diagram:** `GEO-RIGHT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-RIGHT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Subtract when finding a shorter side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — The ladder is the hypotenuse. <!-- MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S1 -->

$$h^2 + 7^2 = 25^2$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S2 -->

$$h^2 = 625 - 49 = 576$$

Step 3 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-01-S3 -->

$$h = 24\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $24\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $24\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S1", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S2", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** A cuboid measures 2 cm × 3 cm × 6 cm. Find the length of the longest rod that fits inside (the space diagonal).

- **Hint 1:** Use Pythagoras twice.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonal of the base. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S1 -->

$$d^2 = 2^2 + 3^2 = 13$$

Step 2 — Space diagonal uses d and the height. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S2 -->

$$D^2 = d^2 + 6^2 = 13 + 36 = 49$$

Step 3 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-01-S3 -->

$$D = 7\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $7\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $7\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

#### MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S1", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S2", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** A cuboid measures 3 cm × 4 cm × 12 cm. Find the length of the longest rod that fits inside (the space diagonal).

- **Hint 1:** Use Pythagoras twice.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonal of the base. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S1 -->

$$d^2 = 3^2 + 4^2 = 25$$

Step 2 — Space diagonal uses d and the height. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S2 -->

$$D^2 = d^2 + 12^2 = 25 + 144 = 169$$

Step 3 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-02-S3 -->

$$D = 13\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $13\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $13\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S1", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S2", "MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** A cuboid measures 6 cm × 8 cm × 24 cm. Find the length of the longest rod that fits inside (the space diagonal).

- **Hint 1:** Use Pythagoras twice.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Diagonal of the base. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S1 -->

$$d^2 = 6^2 + 8^2 = 100$$

Step 2 — Space diagonal uses d and the height. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S2 -->

$$D^2 = d^2 + 24^2 = 100 + 576 = 676$$

Step 3 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-A-PYTH_TWO_S-03-S3 -->

$$D = 26\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $26\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $26\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

#### MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** In right-angled ΔABC, ∠B = 90°, AB = 4 cm, BC = 3 cm and AC = 5 cm. Write sin A, cos A and tan A.

**Diagram:** `GEO-RIGHT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-RIGHT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** SOH CAH TOA.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — For angle A: opposite = BC, adjacent = AB, hypotenuse = AC. <!-- MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S1 -->

$$\text{opp}=BC,\ \text{adj}=AB,\ \text{hyp}=AC$$

Step 2 — Ratios. <!-- MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S2 -->

$$\sin A = \frac{3}{5},\ \cos A = \frac{4}{5},\ \tan A = \frac{3}{4}$$

**Final answer:** $\sin A=\frac{3}{5},\ \cos A=\frac{4}{5},\ \tan A=\frac{3}{4}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\sin A=\frac{3}{5},\ \cos A=\frac{4}{5},\ \tan A=\frac{3}{4}$

#### MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-ELEV-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From a point 20 m from the foot of a vertical tower on level ground, the angle of elevation of the top is 35°. Find the height of the tower. (Use tables: tan 35° = 0.7002)

**Diagram:** `TRI-ELEV-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-ELEV-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Opposite and adjacent → tan.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw a right triangle: height opposite the angle, distance adjacent. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S1 -->

$$\tan 35^\circ = \frac{h}{20}$$

Step 2 — Make h the subject. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S2 -->

$$h = 20\times\tan 35^\circ = 20\times 0.7002$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S3 -->

$$h \approx 14.00\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $14.00\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $14.00\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.05

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-DEPR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S2", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S3", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From the top of a 50 m cliff, the angles of depression of two boats in line with the foot of the cliff are 35° and 60°. Find the distance between the boats. (tan 35° = 0.7002, tan 60° = 1.7321)

**Diagram:** `TRI-DEPR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-DEPR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The smaller angle belongs to the farther boat.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle of depression = angle of elevation from the boat (alternate angles). <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S1 -->

$$\tan\theta = \frac{50}{x}$$

Step 2 — Far boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S2 -->

$$x_1 = \frac{50}{\tan 35^\circ} = \frac{50}{0.7002} \approx 71.41$$

Step 3 — Near boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S3 -->

$$x_2 = \frac{50}{1.7321} \approx 28.87$$

Step 4 — Difference. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S4 -->

$$71.41 - 28.87 \approx 42.54\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $\approx 42.54\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\approx 42.54\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.1

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

$$x_1 = \frac{40}{\tan 30^\circ} = \frac{40}{0.5774} \approx 69.28$$

$$x_2 = \frac{40}{1.1918} \approx 33.56$$

## G11C19 — Matrices

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MAT-ADD", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S1", "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}-1 & 2\\0 & 4\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}2 & 2\\-2 & 2\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}-1+(2) & 2+(2)\\0+(-2) & 4+(2)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}1 & 4\\-2 & 6\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}1 & 4\\-2 & 6\end{pmatrix}$

#### MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-02

**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}1 & -3\\5 & 4\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}4 & 4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-02-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}1+(4) & -3+(4)\\5+(4) & 4+(2)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-02-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}5 & 1\\9 & 6\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}5 & 1\\9 & 6\end{pmatrix}$

**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}3 & -1\\5 & 5\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}4 & -3\\-1 & -3\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}3+(4) & -1+(-3)\\5+(-1) & 5+(-3)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}7 & -4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}7 & -4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-04-S2 -->
