#### MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S1", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S2", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-MAT-MULT`

**Question:** Find AB where $A = \begin{pmatrix}3 & -1\\1 & 0\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}0\\3\end{pmatrix}$. State the order of AB.

- **Hint 1:** Each row of A times the column of B.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Order: (2 × 2)(2 × 1) → 2 × 1. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S1 -->

$$2\times 1$$

Step 2 — Row × column. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix} 3(0) + -1(3) \\ 1(0) + 0(3) \end{pmatrix}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-01-S3 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}-3\\0\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}-3\\0\end{pmatrix}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S1", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S2", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S3"]} -->

**Question:** Find AB where $A = \begin{pmatrix}0 & 0\\0 & 4\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}1\\1\end{pmatrix}$. State the order of AB.

- **Hint 1:** Each row of A times the column of B.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Order: (2 × 2)(2 × 1) → 2 × 1. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S1 -->

$$2\times 1$$

Step 2 — Row × column. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix} 0(1) + 0(1) \\ 0(1) + 4(1) \end{pmatrix}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-02-S3 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}0\\4\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}0\\4\end{pmatrix}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S1", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S2", "MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S3"]} -->

- **Hint 1:** Each row of A times the column of B.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Order: (2 × 2)(2 × 1) → 2 × 1. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S1 -->

$$2\times 1$$

Step 2 — Row × column. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix} 2(-1) + 3(-1) \\ 4(-1) + -1(-1) \end{pmatrix}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-03-S3 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}-5\\-3\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}-5\\-3\end{pmatrix}$

- **Hint 1:** Each row of A times the column of B.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Order: (2 × 2)(2 × 1) → 2 × 1. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-04-S1 -->

$$2\times 1$$

Step 2 — Row × column. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-04-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix} -2(-2) + 2(2) \\ 0(-2) + -2(2) \end{pmatrix}$$

Step 3 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-G-MAT_MULT-04-S3 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}8\\-4\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}8\\-4\end{pmatrix}$

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S1", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S2", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MAT-MULT`

**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 3 & 2 \\ 1 & -1 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 5 \\ -5 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrix multiplication gives two equations.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply out. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S1 -->

$$3x  + 2y = 5,\quad 1x  - 1y = -5$$

Step 2 — Solve the simultaneous equations. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S2 -->

$$x = -1,\ y = 4$$

Step 3 — Check. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01-S3 -->

$$3(-1)  + 2(4) = 5\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x=-1,\ y=4$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=-1,\ y=4$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S1", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S2", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MAT-MULT`

**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 2 & 1 \\ 1 & 3 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 4 \\ -3 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrix multiplication gives two equations.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply out. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S1 -->

$$2x  + 1y = 4,\quad 1x  + 3y = -3$$

Step 2 — Solve the simultaneous equations. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S2 -->

$$x = 3,\ y = -2$$

Step 3 — Check. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02-S3 -->

$$2(3)  + 1(-2) = 4\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x=3,\ y=-2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=3,\ y=-2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S1", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S2", "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MAT-MULT`

**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ 2 & 5 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 7 \\ 18 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrix multiplication gives two equations.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply out. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S1 -->

$$1x  + 2y = 7,\quad 2x  + 5y = 18$$

Step 2 — Solve the simultaneous equations. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S2 -->

$$x = -1,\ y = 4$$

Step 3 — Check. <!-- MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03-S3 -->

$$1(-1)  + 2(4) = 7\ \checkmark$$

**Final answer:** $x=-1,\ y=4$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=-1,\ y=4$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G11C20 — Inequalities

#### MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C20", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-INEQ2", "diagram": "GRA-REGION-01", "kind": "concept", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S1", "MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S2", "MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Inequalities · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-INEQ2`

**Question:** Does the point (1, 2) lie in the region defined by x + y ≤ 5 and y ≥ 1?

**Diagram:** `GRA-REGION-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-REGION-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Substitute the point into each inequality.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Test x + y ≤ 5. <!-- MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S1 -->

$$1+2 = 3 \Rightarrow \checkmark$$

Step 2 — Test y ≥ 1. <!-- MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S2 -->

$$2 \geq 1 \Rightarrow \checkmark$$

Step 3 — Both must be true. <!-- MQ-G11C20-F-POINT_IN_R-01-S3 -->

$$\text{Yes}$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Yes}$

#### MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C20", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-INEQ2", "diagram": "GRA-REGION-01", "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S1", "MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S2", "MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Inequalities · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-INEQ2`

**Question:** Write the inequalities that describe the region bounded by the x-axis, the y-axis and the line x + 2y = 8 (the triangle in the first quadrant), including the boundary.

**Diagram:** `GRA-REGION-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-REGION-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Test the origin to decide which side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Right of the y-axis. <!-- MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S1 -->

$$x \geq 0$$

Step 2 — Above the x-axis. <!-- MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S2 -->

$$y \geq 0$$

Step 3 — Test (0,0) in x + 2y: 0 ≤ 8, so the origin side is x + 2y ≤ 8. <!-- MQ-G11C20-G-REGION_DES-01-S3 -->

$$x + 2y \leq 8$$

**Final answer:** $x\geq 0,\ y\geq 0,\ x+2y\leq 8$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C20", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-INEQ2", "diagram": "GRA-REGION-01", "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S2", "MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S3", "MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Inequalities · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-INEQ2`

**Question:** List all points (x, y) with whole-number coordinates that satisfy x ≥ 1, y ≥ x and x + y ≤ 4.

**Diagram:** `GRA-REGION-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-REGION-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Fix x, then find the allowed y values.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — x starts at 1. <!-- MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S1 -->

$$x = 1, 2, \dots$$

Step 2 — For x = 1: y ≥ 1 and y ≤ 3. <!-- MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S2 -->

$$(1,1),(1,2),(1,3)$$

Step 3 — For x = 2: y ≥ 2 and y ≤ 2. <!-- MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S3 -->

$$(2,2)$$

Step 4 — x = 3 fails (y ≥ 3 and y ≤ 1). <!-- MQ-G11C20-A-SIMULTANEO-01-S4 -->

$$(1,1), (1,2), (1,3), (2,2)$$

**Final answer:** $(1,1), (1,2), (1,3), (2,2)$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G11C21 — Cyclic Quadrilaterals

#### MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S1", "MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. ∠A = 70° and ∠B = 95°. Find ∠C and ∠D.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Opposite angles are supplementary.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S1 -->

$$\angle C = 180 - 70 = 110^\circ$$

Step 2 — Same for B and D. <!-- MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S2 -->

$$\angle D = 180 - 95 = 85^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. Side BC is produced to E. ∠BAD = 85°. Find ∠DCE.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Or: ∠BCD = 180° − ∠A, then ∠DCE = 180° − ∠BCD.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Exterior angle of a cyclic quadrilateral = interior opposite angle. <!-- MQ-G11C21-G-CYCLIC_EXT-01-S1 -->

$$\angle DCE = \angle BAD = 85^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $85^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $85^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S1", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S2", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** In cyclic quadrilateral PQRS, ∠P = $(2x + 10)^\circ$ and ∠R = $(x + 95)^\circ$. Find x and ∠R.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** P and R are opposite.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + (x+95) = 180$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S2 -->

$$3x + 105 = 180 \Rightarrow 3x = 75$$

Step 3 — Solve and substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S3 -->

$$x = 25,\ \angle R = 25+95 = 120^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=25,\ \angle R=120^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=25,\ \angle R=120^\circ$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02-S1", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02-S2", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02-S3"]} -->

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** P and R are opposite.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + (x+65) = 180$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-02-S2 -->

$$3x + 75 = 180 \Rightarrow 3x = 105$$

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S1", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S2", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** In cyclic quadrilateral PQRS, ∠P = $(2x + 10)^\circ$ and ∠R = $(x + 110)^\circ$. Find x and ∠R.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** P and R are opposite.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + (x+110) = 180$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S2 -->

$$3x + 120 = 180 \Rightarrow 3x = 60$$

Step 3 — Solve and substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-03-S3 -->

$$x = 20,\ \angle R = 20+110 = 130^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=20,\ \angle R=130^\circ$

## G11C22 — Tangents

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S1", "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S2", "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-CIR-TAN`

**Question:** A tangent is drawn from a point P to a circle with centre O and radius 5 cm. OP = 13 cm. Find the length of the tangent.

**Diagram:** `GEO-TANGENT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TANGENT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The right angle is at the point of contact.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Tangent ⟂ radius at the point of contact. <!-- MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S1 -->

$$\angle OTP = 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Pythagoras. <!-- MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S2 -->

$$PT^2 = 13^2 - 5^2 = 144$$

Step 3 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01-S3 -->

$$PT = 12\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $12\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $12\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-02", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S1", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S2", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-CIR-TAN`

**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 50°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

**Diagram:** `GEO-TANGENT-02` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TANGENT-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangents from an external point are equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S1 -->

$$90^\circ + 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S2 -->

$$\angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 50 = 130^\circ$$

Step 3 — PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01-S3 -->

$$\angle PAB = \frac{180-50}{2} = 65^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle AOB=130^\circ,\ \angle PAB=65^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle AOB=130^\circ,\ \angle PAB=65^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-02", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S1", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S2", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-CIR-TAN`

**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 40°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

**Diagram:** `GEO-TANGENT-02` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TANGENT-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangents from an external point are equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S1 -->

$$90^\circ + 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S2 -->

$$\angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 40 = 140^\circ$$

Step 3 — PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02-S3 -->

$$\angle PAB = \frac{180-40}{2} = 70^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle AOB=140^\circ,\ \angle PAB=70^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle AOB=140^\circ,\ \angle PAB=70^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-02", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S1", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S2", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S3"]} -->

**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 64°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

**Diagram:** `GEO-TANGENT-02` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TANGENT-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangents from an external point are equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S1 -->

$$90^\circ + 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S2 -->

$$\angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 64 = 116^\circ$$

Step 3 — PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-03-S3 -->

$$\angle PAB = \frac{180-64}{2} = 58^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle AOB=116^\circ,\ \angle PAB=58^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle AOB=116^\circ,\ \angle PAB=58^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

#### MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-02", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S1", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S2", "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-CIR-TAN`

**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 70°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

**Diagram:** `GEO-TANGENT-02` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-TANGENT-02.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangents from an external point are equal.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S1 -->

$$90^\circ + 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S2 -->

$$\angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 70 = 110^\circ$$

Step 3 — PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. <!-- MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-04-S3 -->

$$\angle PAB = \frac{180-70}{2} = 55^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle AOB=110^\circ,\ \angle PAB=55^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle AOB=110^\circ,\ \angle PAB=55^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-ALTSEG", "diagram": "GEO-ALTSEG-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S2"]} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-ALTSEG`

**Question:** TA is a tangent to a circle at A. AB and AC are chords. ∠TAB = 55° and ∠ABC = 65°. Find ∠ACB and ∠BAC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-ALTSEG-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ALTSEG-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the angle on the OTHER side of the chord AB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Alternate segment theorem: angle between tangent and chord = angle in the alternate segment. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ACB = \angle TAB = 55^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angle sum of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S2 -->

$$\angle BAC = 180 - 55 - 65 = 60^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle ACB=55^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle ACB=55^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-ALTSEG", "diagram": "GEO-ALTSEG-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S1", "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S2"]} -->

**Diagram:** `GEO-ALTSEG-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ALTSEG-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the angle on the OTHER side of the chord AB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Alternate segment theorem: angle between tangent and chord = angle in the alternate segment. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S1 -->

$$\angle ACB = \angle TAB = 40^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angle sum of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S2 -->

$$\angle BAC = 180 - 40 - 80 = 60^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle ACB=40^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$

## G11C23 — Constructions

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "C-TANGENT", "diagram": "CON-TANGENT-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `C-TANGENT`

**Question:** Draw a circle of radius 3 cm with centre O and mark a point A on it. Construct the tangent at A. Write the steps.

**Diagram:** `CON-TANGENT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-TANGENT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangent is perpendicular to the radius.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw the circle, radius 3 cm, and mark A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$OA = r$$

Step 2 — Join OA and extend it beyond A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$OA\ \text{extended}$$

Step 3 — Construct a perpendicular to OA at A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$\perp\ \text{at }A$$

Step 4 — That perpendicular is the tangent. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$\text{tangent}\perp OA$$

**Final answer:** $\text{tangent at }A\perp OA$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "C-CIRCUM", "diagram": "CON-CIRCUM-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `C-CIRCUM`

**Question:** Construct triangle ABC with AB = 6 cm, BC = 7 cm and AC = 5 cm, then construct its circumcircle. Write the steps.

**Diagram:** `CON-CIRCUM-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-CIRCUM-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The circumcentre is equidistant from all three vertices.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw BC = 7 cm. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$BC = 7$$

Step 2 — Arc radius 6 cm from B and arc radius 5 cm from C meet at A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$A$$

Step 3 — Construct the perpendicular bisectors of two sides. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$\text{bisectors of }AB,\ BC$$

Step 4 — They meet at O (circumcentre). Draw the circle with radius OA. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$OA = OB = OC$$

**Final answer:** $\text{circle centre }O,\ \text{radius }OA$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "C-INCIRCLE", "diagram": "CON-ANGBIS-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `C-INCIRCLE`

**Question:** Construct an equilateral triangle of side 6 cm and construct its inscribed circle (incircle). Write the steps and measure the radius.

**Diagram:** `CON-ANGBIS-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-ANGBIS-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The incentre is where the angle bisectors meet.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw AB = 6 cm; arcs of 6 cm from A and B meet at C. <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$AB = BC = CA = 6$$

Step 2 — Bisect two angles (e.g. ∠A and ∠B). <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$\text{bisectors meet at } I$$

Step 3 — Draw a perpendicular from I to a side; this is the radius. <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$r = ID$$

Step 4 — Expected radius (check by calculation). <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$r = \frac{6}{2\sqrt3} \approx 1.73\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $r\approx 1.7\ \text{cm}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G11C24 — Sets

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C24", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "concept", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S1", "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S2", "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S3"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** U = {1, 2, …, 9}, A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}, B = {4, 5, 6, 7}. Find A ∩ B, A ∪ B and A′.

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** ∩ = and, ∪ = or, ′ = not.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Intersection: elements in both. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S1 -->

$$A\cap B = \{4,5\}$$

Step 2 — Union: elements in either. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S2 -->

$$A\cup B = \{1,2,3,4,5,6,7\}$$

Step 3 — Complement: in U but not in A. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S3 -->

$$A' = \{6,7,8,9\}$$

**Final answer:** $A\cap B=\{4,5\};\ A\cup B=\{1,\dots,7\};\ A'=\{6,7,8,9\}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### Grade Improvement

#### MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C24", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION3", "diagram": "SET-VENN3-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S1", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S2", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S3", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S4", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION3`

**Question:** In a group of 50, 20 study Maths (M), 22 Science (S) and 18 English (E). 8 study M and S, 6 study S and E, 5 study M and E, and 3 study all three. Find (i) the number who study only Maths (ii) the number who study none.

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN3-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN3-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Always fill a Venn diagram from the centre outward.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Centre region (all three). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S1 -->

$$n(M\cap S\cap E) = 3$$

Step 2 — Exactly two subjects (subtract the centre). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S2 -->

$$MS: 8-3=5,\ SE: 6-3=3,\ ME: 5-3=2$$

Step 3 — Only Maths. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S3 -->

$$20 - 5 - 2 - 3 = 10$$

Step 4 — Only S and only E similarly. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S4 -->

$$11,\ 10$$

Step 5 — None = total − all regions. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S5 -->

$$50 - 44 = 6$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C24", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION3", "diagram": "SET-VENN3-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S1", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S2", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S3", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S4", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION3`

**Question:** In a group of 40, 15 study Maths (M), 18 Science (S) and 16 English (E). 5 study M and S, 4 study S and E, 6 study M and E, and 2 study all three. Find (i) the number who study only Maths (ii) the number who study none.

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN3-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN3-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Always fill a Venn diagram from the centre outward.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Centre region (all three). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S1 -->

$$n(M\cap S\cap E) = 2$$

Step 2 — Exactly two subjects (subtract the centre). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S2 -->

$$MS: 5-2=3,\ SE: 4-2=2,\ ME: 6-2=4$$

Step 3 — Only Maths. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S3 -->

$$15 - 3 - 4 - 2 = 6$$

Step 4 — Only S and only E similarly. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S4 -->

$$11,\ 8$$

Step 5 — None = total − all regions. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02-S5 -->

$$40 - 36 = 4$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 6\quad(ii)\ 4$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 6\quad(ii)\ 4$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Step-by-step solution**

## G11C25 — Probability

### Foundation Mastery

#### MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-INDEP", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-01-S1"]} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-PRO-INDEP`

**Question:** Events A and B are independent with P(A) = \frac{3}{4} and P(B) = \frac{2}{3}. Find P(A and B).

- **Hint 1:** 'And' along a branch means multiply.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Independent → multiply. <!-- MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-01-S1 -->

$$P(A\cap B) = \frac{3}{4}\times\frac{2}{3} = \frac{1}{2}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{1}{2}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{1}{2}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

Step 1 — Independent → multiply. <!-- MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-02-S1 -->

$$P(A\cap B) = \frac{1}{2}\times\frac{1}{6} = \frac{1}{12}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{1}{12}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{1}{12}$

#### MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S1", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S2", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S3", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S4", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S5"]} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** Bag 1 has 2 red and 3 blue balls; Bag 2 has 1 red and 3 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Multiply along branches, add between outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — First branches (Bag 1). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S1 -->

$$P(R_1)=\frac{2}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{3}{5}$$

Step 2 — Second branches (Bag 2). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S2 -->

$$P(R_2)=\frac{1}{4},\ P(B_2)=\frac{3}{4}$$

Step 3 — Both red: multiply along the branch. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{4} = \frac{1}{10}$$

Step 4 — Both blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{3}{5}\times\frac{3}{4} = \frac{9}{20}$$

Step 5 — Same colour: add the two outcomes. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S5 -->

$$\frac{1}{10} + \frac{9}{20} = \frac{11}{20}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{11}{20}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{11}{20}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### A Grade Mastery

#### MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S2", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S3", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** A box has 4 red and 4 blue pens. Two pens are taken one after the other WITHOUT replacement. Find the probability that the two pens are of different colours.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Without replacement: the second fraction changes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Second draw has one fewer pen. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S1 -->

$$\text{total}: 8 \to 7$$

Step 2 — Red then blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{4}{8}\times\frac{4}{7} = \frac{2}{7}$$

Step 3 — Blue then red. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{4}{8}\times\frac{4}{7} = \frac{2}{7}$$

Step 4 — Add. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{2}{7} + \frac{2}{7} = \frac{4}{7}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{4}{7}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{4}{7}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S1", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S2", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S3", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** A box has 5 red and 3 blue pens. Two pens are taken one after the other WITHOUT replacement. Find the probability that the two pens are of different colours.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Without replacement: the second fraction changes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Second draw has one fewer pen. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S1 -->

$$\text{total}: 8 \to 7$$

Step 2 — Red then blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{5}{8}\times\frac{3}{7} = \frac{15}{56}$$

Step 3 — Blue then red. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S3 -->

$$\frac{3}{8}\times\frac{5}{7} = \frac{15}{56}$$

Step 4 — Add. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-02-S4 -->

$$\frac{15}{56} + \frac{15}{56} = \frac{15}{28}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{15}{28}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{15}{28}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S1", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S2", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S3", "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S4"]} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** A box has 6 red and 2 blue pens. Two pens are taken one after the other WITHOUT replacement. Find the probability that the two pens are of different colours.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Without replacement: the second fraction changes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Second draw has one fewer pen. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S1 -->

$$\text{total}: 8 \to 7$$

Step 2 — Red then blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S2 -->

$$\frac{6}{8}\times\frac{2}{7} = \frac{3}{14}$$

Step 3 — Blue then red. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S3 -->

$$\frac{2}{8}\times\frac{6}{7} = \frac{3}{14}$$

Step 4 — Add. <!-- MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-03-S4 -->

$$\frac{3}{14} + \frac{3}{14} = \frac{3}{7}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{3}{7}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{3}{7}$
